BREP RAYTRACE IMPLEMENTATION CURRENT STATE
==========================================

Last updated: 2026-10-08
Repository: brlcad
Implementation specification: brep_raytrace_plan.txt
Active objective: Implement the complete specification, preserving this file
as the authoritative continuation log until all acceptance gates pass.


CURRENT PHASE
-------------

2026-10-08 REBASED REVIEW SERIES, LOW-DISTORTION PROFILING, AND QUALIFICATION

The complete B-Rep raytracing work is now rebased directly on fetched
`origin/main` commit `e7c61497df`.  The review branch contains 30 ordered
commits: topology and test groundwork; bounded allocation-free root/seam
isolation; direction-dependent grazing and event certificates; topology-aware
partition cleanup; compact prepared data; certified cell routing; scene-wide
pack and persistent prep caches; audit/oracle coverage; whole-render tests;
low-distortion route profiling; reproducible benchmarks; and this status
record.  Each commit message states both the mechanism and why it improves
correctness, boundedness, memory, or speed.  There are no fixup/squash/WIP
commits or uncommitted source changes.  The pre-rewrite, pre-autosquash,
pre-origin-rebase, and final-pre-rewrite safety refs remain available under
`safety/`.

After the rebase and history rewrite, a full Release Ninja rebuild completed
successfully with the MSVC 14.44 toolchain.  The pre-profiler focused
`(brep|cache)` matrix passed 118/118 tests in 637.32 seconds.  The completed
profiling change was then rebuilt successfully; its direct-render and profile-
histogram tests pass.  The status-2 proxy test continues to expose a narrow,
load-sensitive throughput boundary: an isolated run passed at 0.9579/0.9584
wall/CPU throughput relative to branch legacy, and a later isolated run passed
at 0.9872/0.9872, while adjacent three-test runs measured as low as
0.9263/0.9307.  Its correctness and parameter-mapping checks pass in every
run.  This timing gate needs a quiet-host methodology; it is not a compile or
routing-correctness failure.

`brlcad_build/gta02-exact-ray-oracle-final-history-v1` reruns the
production-shaped accuracy gate from the rebuilt binaries.  All ten selective
corrections pass in both ray directions, including transformed instances and
the independent analytic torus root check; all three non-corrective prepared
completions match both depth-3 and depth-8 legacy transactions.  The summary
SHA-256 is
`44FCC36B793E0889EC2F0768FC394A666252E6D020C2F14FE105A7B7BB413356`.

The new seven-trial archive
`brlcad_build/gta02-paired-current-main-v1` compares the rebuilt review tip
with a clean Release/Ninja build of the exact current base `e7c61497df`.
At 1,024 square, 24 workers, light model 2, depth 3, and cache disabled, every
lane is internally deterministic.  Routed/legacy paired medians are 1.1940x
CPU and 1.1904x wall, retaining the agreed <=1.20x release envelope.  Against
current main, the paired medians are 1.1694x CPU and 1.1752x wall for SHOT.
The independently selected routed/main medians are 122.109/104.578 CPU
seconds and 5.42947/4.66426 wall seconds.  Thus routed interrogation provides
85.6 percent of main CPU throughput and 85.9 percent of main wall throughput;
it is comparable but has not reached the <=1.10x optimization target.

Preparation remains a real, bounded cost rather than the former eager-pack
failure.  Routed median prep wall time is 15.10894 seconds versus 12.33541 on
current main; the paired ratio is 1.2199x.  The same routed prep is only
1.0047x branch legacy, showing that routing-pack construction is not the
source of the branch/main gap.  Median routed prep-plus-shot wall time is
20.53841 seconds versus 16.99892 for current main and 19.57823 for branch
legacy.  Paired medians are 1.2078x main and 1.0473x branch legacy.  Repeated
frames amortize prep and see the 16.9--17.5 percent interrogation premium;
single-frame jobs see approximately 20.8 percent end-to-end overhead.

All routed images repeat SHA-256
`FD626C13AF6A8171DD405DE5044949903A0681CDAD144F152A6D1159B15143D4`;
all branch-legacy images repeat
`C16C4D1160E90F0ABA3282D3CD77D9C293223B33432CE80A585FB124FA47D957`;
and all current-main images repeat
`09D47BBAB48383BC237D85CCD2EA7F2BD2CE962C4C0AD64D0443E10EA52A8C5B`.
Main's different hit/image population is a comparison, not the correctness
oracle; the exact-ray checks above independently establish the ten routed
corrections.  Archive manifest/results/summary SHA-256 values are
`734A8A9D2409A0B3B36EDA4CEE076FCF651FA2832C62E74622FE60802BECAA01`,
`CD5CBF3C1FF30C469A66F64259F57A764A85613CCE5F57E3CF7FF1FBD6A8094D`,
and `5A1786B61A951C91D58C1D952306B6969B5A0A84EBCE4667301F321FCAD73C70`.
The exact benchmarked rt/librt/brep-audit SHA-256 values are
`ADE2BD250407F17F3ABDA18A31F914B3FE8B9828807B07A79CDE85DD2F2ECF77`,
`D843E5000F75678C8072DB08C60A0CE2971BF2D8FB66AC188F35C98646AC499C`,
and `70FD57A27B68D5995FA287779E8699D0B71C8616EED5A53CA28A12A1609219DF`.
The final post-rewrite build produced rt/librt/brep-audit SHA-256 values
`A1C6108CC3D9C9D7E5F80AD49E7A21DA38D9954CEDDE57BB2FD9526555B5D846`,
`A85E87E8560655F2BC4615EC68A0683FAE9565B433305C5DEBD212708233A491`,
and `1DE285E9610E47060C94EB1CA6D95578F5E7FEA14FC9B3101E7DE4565934CE19`;
the final-history oracle above binds its result to the latter two binaries.

Route profiling no longer contends on process-wide atomics.  Each
`a_resource->re_cpu` writes a cache-line-separated fixed slot, the report
merges slots only after rendering, and shadow traces use inline thread-local
storage rather than a first-shot allocation.  Exact counters remain enabled
for every profiled shot; six mutually exclusive phase clocks use staggered
deterministic 1-in-64 sampling.  Serial and two-worker renderer tests verify
identical images, identical shot/lane populations, and exact phase accounting.
A seven-trial alternating 1,024-square 63-face run measured profiled/unprofiled
paired medians of 1.0173x CPU and 1.0147x wall, within the two-percent
measurement-distortion gate.  Phase storage is kept out of the returned
production routing decision, preserving its unprofiled hot-path layout.

The final unprofiled 63-face archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-final-history-multiface-v1`.  Over seven alternating
trials, the routed path reaches 441,505.68 CPU and 438,594.16 wall
rays/second, versus 232,210.60 and 229,839.74 on current main: 1.901x and
1.908x main throughput.  It retains 91.4 percent of same-branch legacy CPU
throughput and 91.7 percent of wall throughput; paired routed/legacy time
medians are 1.1007x CPU and 1.0918x wall.  All routed/legacy ray counts, hit
aggregates, and result digests agree.  Manifest/results/summary SHA-256 values
are `DDE73E80CFE605609FEF51B42EC6FE5FA3B899FA1C7A961198D248410A4492DD`,
`15BB6DB35E017A1B651F1C9342555CEB3C2E976F95F4CDEF43DBC1260507F92A`,
and `990E9F120861D741BB6ECD7922BF8AE4686EEB10C2033E96DF16F13549AF31BD`.
Its manifest records clean source commit `6fbbd88e29`; the subsequent final
commit amendment changes only this documentation, not the tested code or
binaries.

A 1,024-square, 24-worker GTA02 profile covers 3,833,367 B-Rep shots.  Its
1-in-64 aggregate estimates rank the measured route work as 42.081 seconds in
immutable cell/span traversal, 11.339 seconds in selective replay, 7.562
seconds in root certification, 3.689 seconds in eligibility/dispatch, 0.391
seconds in grazing continuation, and 0.289 seconds in direction-cache work.
The absolute values are sampled aggregate CPU time, not render wall time, but
the ranking is actionable: cache lookup/build is not the present bottleneck.
Optimize the repeated cell/span walk first, then replay admission and the root
certificate; do not trade away the direction-dependent grazing proof.

The local conclusion therefore remains unchanged on the current upstream
base: there is no fundamental hierarchy-depth, face-count, cache-memory, or
grazing-method blocker.  The implementation is a demonstrated production-
viable opt-in with bounded memory, no shot-time certificate/replay allocation,
and real accuracy gains.  Default-on status still requires non-MSVC sanitizer,
race/leak, second-compiler, and additional approved production-corpus gates.


2026-10-07 MULTIVIEW ORACLE AND PAIRED GTA RELEASE-GATE CHECKPOINT

The final local evidence closes the accuracy and trace-time questions that can
be answered on this host.  `brlcad_build/gta02-exact-ray-oracle-v4` now
independently validates ten selective corrections in both ray directions,
using the actual hierarchy transforms for instanced solids.  The original
five default-view cases remain, and reverse-oblique cases 180, 269, 1376,
59287, and 61814 add five more.  Three non-corrective general-prepared
completions (axis-Z 31 and 13636, and reverse-oblique 2730) reproduce both the
depth-3 and depth-8 legacy hit transactions.  They demonstrate that the
general completion lane can finish safely without asserting that every
completion must change the legacy answer.  The oracle summary SHA-256 is
`729AA2DE7FDDBDECF26FB1DCDE7BE719CF80F1613B894EB0E584B29C60BA8C1A`.

The MFM case 59287 no longer depends only on a deeper numerical reference.
`brep-audit --mesh-ray-face` recognizes face 188's analytic torus and solves
the ray/torus quartic independently.  Its two real roots are
4.485271413185533 and 4.576743517733146.  The routed endpoints are
4.485271412841947 and 4.576743518900628, differing by at most 1.2e-9, and
both UV endpoints are safely inside the face trim.  This is direct evidence
that the near-tangent pair is real and that the selective result is more
accurate than the depth-3 miss.

Four additional 1,024-square whole-assembly views exercise the complete CSG
hierarchy.  Axis X makes 996 optional attempts and no completion; axis Y makes
12,588 and no completion; axis Z makes 13,663 attempts and two equivalent
general completions; reverse oblique makes 62,012 attempts, five level-1
selective corrections, and one equivalent general completion.  Reversal
mismatches remain zero.  Routed and branch-legacy final images are byte
identical in all four views; publications in these views either reproduce the
legacy primitive transaction or are hidden by the enclosing CSG result.  The
default view remains the separate, visible one-pixel correction documented
below.  Exact instance transforms are now recorded in route-detail logs and
accepted by `brep-audit --transform`, so these cases are reproducible in local
object coordinates rather than approximated as identity instances.

The final paired whole-assembly timing archive is
`brlcad_build/gta02-paired-final-v1`.  It uses seven alternating measured
trials at 1,024 square, 24 workers, light model 2, and SurfaceTree depth 3.
The routed/legacy median of paired CPU-time ratios is 1.1979x and the paired
wall-time median is 1.1612x; both explicit <=1.20x release gates pass.  The
ratio of independently selected medians is 122.000/102.281 = 1.1928x for CPU
and 6.26168/5.35411 = 1.1695x for wall.  All seven routed images repeat SHA-256
`FD626C13AF6A8171DD405DE5044949903A0681CDAD144F152A6D1159B15143D4`;
all seven legacy images repeat
`C16C4D1160E90F0ABA3282D3CD77D9C293223B33432CE80A585FB124FA47D957`.
The expected five-hit difference is stable.

Against clean main in that archive, routed SHOT CPU time is 122.000 versus
105.391 seconds (1.158x, or 86.4 percent of main's CPU throughput).  The wall
medians are 6.26168 versus 6.08644 seconds (1.029x, or 97.2 percent of main's
wall throughput), but wall trials range widely because of host scheduling and
must not be overinterpreted.  The earlier quieter v2 archive remains the
end-to-end reference: 19.004 seconds routed prep-plus-shot versus 18.015 for
branch legacy and 15.792 for main.  Together with 1.95x main throughput on the
63-face fixture, this shows no fundamental face-count or hierarchy blocker:
the remaining cost is bounded common-path routing work, not uniform depth-8
construction or unbounded cache growth.

The final archive manifest/results/summary SHA-256 values are
`1E685117A90553CF1D02EAB383D1570E42FA0DDFBDC3548C2096E84842BD8547`,

The post-change serial focused matrix passes 103/103 tests in 1,478.81
seconds.  `regress-licenses`, `regress-repository`, and `git diff --check`
also pass; the diff check emits only expected Windows line-ending warnings.
The implementation is therefore locally production-performance viable and

GCC, Clang, WSL, or Docker toolchain is installed on this machine, so those

2026-10-07 STAGED GRAZING CONTINUATION AND GTA02 PRODUCTION CHECKPOINT

The directional grazing path now uses staged fixed-workspace traversal rather
than either uniform depth 8 or an unconditional 1,024-box pass.  Every
ray-visible ambiguous span starts with the established 256-box stage.  If work
remains and the configured hard limit is larger, traversal resumes from the
existing stack and may continue to 1,024 boxes; it does not restart, enlarge
the stack, or allocate.  An explicit
`LIBRT_BREP_DIRECTIONAL_GRAZING_BOX_BUDGET` of 256 or less retains the former
single-stage fail-closed behavior.  The default hard limit is now 1,024.

The fixed 128-square GTA profile has 15,377 probes.  Only two cross the first
stage, use 46 continuation boxes, and resolve; there are no grazing triggers or
overflows.  The full 1,024-square profile has 987,879 probes.  Only 120 shot
decisions continue, using 5,853 boxes to resolve 247 spans.  Six spans remain
ambiguous and conservatively trigger fallback; none exhausts the 1,024-box
workspace.  The same full run has 3,833,367 B-Rep calls, 14,976 selective
refinement attempts, five complete level-1 replays, ten recovered hits, zero
reversal mismatches, and five atomic publications.  Its image SHA-256 is
`FD626C13AF6A8171DD405DE5044949903A0681CDAD144F152A6D1159B15143D4`.

The new reproducible GTA exact-ray oracle independently checks all five
publications.  For each ray, depth-3 legacy returns zero hits in both
directions, the audit-only depth-8 reference returns one two-boundary interval,
and the production depth-3 level-1 replay returns the same face pair and
interval in both directions.  The largest endpoint difference between the
deep reference and production replay is 7.02e-8; every fixed acceptance window
is at most 3e-5 wide, well inside the model tolerance.  The cases are MCB ray
529 (faces 0/0), MCF rays 1717 (38/44), 2217 (44/44), and 3312 (44/44), and
MFM ray 12153 (188/188).  The corrected visible MCB pixel and all five segment
corrections are therefore demonstrated accuracy gains, not assumptions that
main is universally correct.

The post-rebuild 63-face seven-trial archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-staged-final-multiface-v1`.  Routed throughput is
441,505.68 CPU / 432,887.67 wall rays per second, branch legacy is
486,296.12 / 475,761.73, and clean main is 226,719.14 / 222,242.80.  The route
is 194.7/194.8 percent of main and 90.8/91.0 percent of branch legacy, or
10.1/9.9 percent extra same-branch CPU/wall time.  Every routed/legacy ray,
intersection, hit/miss aggregate, and result digest agrees.  Manifest/results/
summary SHA-256 values are
`7278A1511AE5C3243D57661E62973F2EE26105DFFD452B4B24EA8910DAFA3ABD`,
`82BD7D6D9554FC70569B733161034BAFF44164977B270FA59F7A3158F6BA9493`,
and `1DE23F1317C2734C82A5C9A0A4985EA3040D685AFD9FFCC5BE7DED73DFACA2AB`.

Two independent seven-trial whole-assembly archives now locate the production
cost boundary.  `gta02-staged-continuation-final-v1` has routed SHOT medians of
121.781 CPU / 6.14339 wall seconds versus branch legacy at 102.281 / 5.11279,
or 19.07/20.16 percent extra time.  The quieter rebuilt-binary repeat,
`gta02-staged-continuation-final-v2`, has 112.109 / 5.16334 versus
94.0781 / 4.14293.  Its CPU ratio is 1.1917; its raw wall ratio of medians is
1.2463 because host stalls move the two medians to different trials, while the
median of the seven paired wall ratios is 1.1987.  The paired CPU ratio is
1.1924.  Thus CPU reproducibly meets the agreed 20-percent envelope and wall
time lies on that boundary rather than showing a structural gap.

In the rebuilt repeat, routed total prep (GETTREE plus pack PREP) is 13.785
wall seconds, branch legacy is 13.904, and main is 11.554.  Median complete
prep-plus-shot latency is 19.004 seconds routed, 18.015 branch legacy, and
15.792 main.  Routing itself adds no prep penalty; the exact branch metadata
cost makes total routed latency 20.34 percent above main and 5.49 percent above
same-branch legacy in this archive.  This is far removed from the obsolete
65--68 second per-object eager-pack experiment.

The memory result is also stable.  Three 128-square samples at 0, 8, and
64 MiB scene-pack budgets have median peak working sets of 1,644,572,672,
1,651,855,360, and 1,721,278,464 bytes.  Clean main uses 5,286,113,280 bytes.
The 8 MiB default is 1.538 GiB, 31.25 percent of main, for a 68.75-percent
saving.  The 64 MiB setting adds material prep and memory without a measured
trace benefit, so weighted 8 MiB remains the production setting.

The final Ninja build succeeds.  In the serial 103-test route/cache matrix,
102 tests passed immediately.  The sole failure was a stale log-count
assertion: after the audit fix made the reference lane a real no-publication
route, the two ray directions are exercised in both lanes and correctly report
four event-class rejections rather than two.  The assertion is updated and its
isolated rerun passes.  A final five-test critical routing/cache subset passes,
as do `regress-licenses`, `regress-repository`, and `git diff --check` (the last
reports only expected Windows line-ending notices).  The provenance-recording
oracle archive is `brlcad_build/gta02-exact-ray-oracle-v3`; its summary SHA-256
is `35A27FE92D25609FDFC4317EEBAD764AE8E2D50DADD0001F2BD747CED7B58EAC`.
Current `rt.exe`/`librt.dll`/`brep-audit.exe` SHA-256 values are
`10CEFD1FC5F67E6023C00F6BB61DB9A3E3794BE0A9681E02D84B16FE27734964`,
`11FF9AA10856D3381EE09222EA6C24FF672ABD115E2D68BA808A63049C0EDA78`,
and `25B1D96401AA0608C0745D5FA066E294D9925CA94287B8A7A830A00BDCE40778`.

The engineering conclusion is that there is no fundamental face-count,
hierarchy-depth, prep, cache-memory, or grazing-certification blocker.  The
current route demonstrates bounded memory, allocation-free shot-time
certification/replay, real-model accuracy corrections, near-main whole-scene
latency, and almost twice main's throughput on the 63-face fixture.  It remains
opt-in pending sanitizer/thread/leak qualification, broader multi-view and
production-model oracles, and an explicit decision on the approximately
20-percent repeated-frame throughput boundary.


2026-10-06 WEIGHTED SCENE CACHE AND FINAL QUALIFICATION

The scene-wide routing-pack ceiling is retained, but equal per-object shares
have been replaced by deterministic weighted water filling.  Each eligible
B-Rep is weighted by the mean projected area of its prepared bounding box
(`dx*dy + dy*dz + dz*dx`), inexpensive objects are fully funded, and the
remaining bytes are divided among larger objects subject to their exact
eligible-byte caps.  Within each object, correction cells are packed in
descending projected-area order.  The policy is view independent: no camera
direction enters persistent prep state.  A zero, sub-minimum, partial, or
exhausted share still uses the exact fixed-workspace reconstruction path, so
cache admission cannot change authority or allocate during a shot.

The allocator now also guards every size_t subtraction against floating-point
apportionment roundoff, computes weight ratios before multiplying by a byte
budget to avoid intermediate overflow, skips cell collection/sorting when an
object cannot retain even the smallest pack, and reports actual constructed
bytes rather than masking them with the estimate.  Stable input order breaks
equal fractional remainders.  The global logical payload remains bounded by
`LIBRT_BREP_ROUTING_PACK_CACHE_BYTES`; retained vector capacity and allocator
overhead remain part of the process-memory measurement gate rather than being
misrepresented as payload bytes.

On the fixed GTA02 128-square profile, weighted 8 MiB uses 8,242,688 of
8,388,608 payload bytes across 123 of 306 B-Reps and spends 0.73419 wall
seconds in the separate pack PREP phase.  Routing decision/directional/
missing-root-certificate times are 284.786/237.975/30.900 milliseconds.  The
same binary before weighting measured 320.700/241.013/64.212 milliseconds at
8 MiB, while an equal-share 64 MiB run measured 278.420/223.015/40.065
milliseconds and spent 3.56921 seconds in pack PREP.  Thus weighted 8 MiB
nearly matches the 64 MiB decision time and beats its certificate time using
one eighth of the configured payload.  A zero-pack control measured
325.010/241.887/67.645 milliseconds and 0.01826 seconds of pack PREP.

The weighted full GTA02 run retains the real accuracy correction.  In seven
alternating 1024-square routed/legacy pairs, every routed image has SHA-256
`FD626C13AF6A8171DD405DE5044949903A0681CDAD144F152A6D1159B15143D4`
and every legacy image has
`C16C4D1160E90F0ABA3282D3CD77D9C293223B33432CE80A585FB124FA47D957`.
Each run has 3,833,533 B-Rep calls; routed records 2,265,993 hits versus
2,265,988 for legacy.  The five recovered intersections come from the five
complete, reversal-consistent level-1 replays, and the visible MCB correction
still agrees with clean main.  This is a demonstrated accuracy gain.  It does
not establish that all 46,325 main-versus-branch normal-pixel differences are
branch improvements; those remain subject to the independent ray oracle.

On the loaded host, the same seven-pair GTA series has routed SHOT medians of
120.453 CPU / 6.80227 wall seconds and branch-legacy medians of 99.625 /
5.43412: 20.9 percent CPU and 25.2 percent wall overhead.  GETTREE medians are
292.266 CPU / 18.43287 wall for routed and 289.281 / 18.44263 for legacy, so
routing mode does not create the prep difference.  The wide wall-time ranges
make this a conservative loaded-host result, not the quiet release archive;
it puts CPU essentially at the agreed 20-percent ceiling but does not pass
both gates.  The earlier 68.24-percent peak-working-set saving and confirmed
accuracy correction make this small premium potentially acceptable, but a
waiver must be based on the final quiet archive rather than selected trials.

The final post-hardening one-CPU 1024-square 63-face archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-pack-weighted-multiface-v2`.  Routed throughput is
453,438.27 CPU / 443,145.23 wall rays per second, versus branch legacy at
493,447.53 / 482,339.20 and clean main at 237,133.80 / 232,566.43.  Routing is
therefore 191.2/190.5 percent of main and 91.9/91.9 percent of branch legacy:
about 8.8 percent same-branch time overhead.  Every routed/legacy ray,
intersection, hit/miss aggregate, and result digest matches.  The manifest,
results, and summary SHA-256 values are
`82DA3CDCEF1DBB1C028838AD24B4FE589B92D3843C0882DEA4820EF8A8DD6A53`/
`04E2C30232D17178923D83B17EBDD952389F665885B1849905B7848DACEF6D99`/
`EE82696D6D3CAE06E8196CECFA6F899700F2538D4E46D68EB3D2382724F3A907`.

A matching seven-trial 1,024-box grazing run produced the same 91.4-percent
CPU/wall near-legacy ratio as its adjacent 256-box run and no output change.
On GTA profiling, 1,024 reduces ambiguity/overflow from 125/119 to 6/0 and
optional work from 4.363 to 3.314 aggregate seconds, but increases the
pathological per-span work ceiling fourfold.  Since it provides no 63-face
throughput benefit, 256 remains the production default; 1,024 stays an
explicit qualification setting.  A future two-stage continuation may spend
the larger bound only after a 256-box ambiguity when the avoided fallback is
known to justify it.

Two direction-cache experiments are explicitly rejected and reverted.  A
build-coalescing mutex made the 128-square routing decision slower rather than
eliminating meaningful duplicate work, and a 512-by-512 direction grid raised
the same profiled decision to 1.573 seconds while increasing retained grid
storage.  Keep the 256-by-256 grid and lock-free fail-closed builder path.

The optimized final source builds successfully.  The complete serial focused
matrix passes 103/103 tests in 1,377.95 seconds after the allocator hardening,
including cache depth/concurrency/corruption/migration, all status-2
throughput gates, incomplete-transaction recovery, all routing-truth and
reversal cases, the scene-byte bound, and the direct render comparison.  The
scene-cache test was then extended with a one-byte sub-minimum budget; its
separate final rerun passes, constructs zero packs, and reproduces the 1 MiB
test image.  `regress-licenses` and `regress-repository` also pass.  Current
`rt.exe`/`librt.dll`/`brep-audit.exe` SHA-256 values are
`C0F29212E54A2DCBA691AA48992017A50B04597C3C81FDBB0F19EC43BD69C1CB`/
`4CDC971312AA926EC8AC9F12EE80C7455938EBB6D42B7AA337ED79778C15E74F`/
`4FBD8C0F7632214E09CAF4223C85B2D32948D9F4565B68F84A5956FFCC2AA7F5`.

The production conclusion is now precise: there is no fundamental 63-face,
prep-scaling, hierarchy-depth, or cache-memory blocker, and there is one
confirmed real-model accuracy improvement.  Default-on release still needs a
quiet whole-assembly timing archive, independent adjudication of every
published GTA ray, broader production corpora, and sanitizer/thread/leak
qualification.


2026-10-06 CERTIFIED INCOMPLETE-TRANSACTION RECOVERY

The current branch now demonstrates one concrete GTA02 accuracy correction,
not merely synthetic correction capability.  The fixed 1024-square, 35/25
surface-normal view at SurfaceTree depth 3 contains 3,833,533 B-Rep calls.
The legacy transaction is incomplete on 19,913 calls.  A complete positive
cell/span certificate selects 14,986 bounded replay attempts; five complete
at refinement level 1, recover ten ordered boundary hits, and have zero
forward/reverse mismatches.  Together with four independently completed
general prepared transactions, nine optional results publish atomically.

The visible correction is pixel (452,230) on
`GTC01_A_MCB01_PRT_item.s`.  Branch legacy returns background there; the
current route returns normal RGB (40,190,196), exactly matching clean main.
The recovered interval is approximately 135.1831815864--135.7898724698.
The current routed image is deterministic with SHA-256
`FD626C13AF6A8171DD405DE5044949903A0681CDAD144F152A6D1159B15143D4`;
branch legacy is
`C16C4D1160E90F0ABA3282D3CD77D9C293223B33432CE80A585FB124FA47D957`.
They differ at exactly that one pixel.  A separate MFM replay also completes
but does not change the final visible pixel, so that case remains a diagnostic
near miss rather than claimed image improvement.

The main comparison is now 46,325 of 1,048,576 normal pixels, not the obsolete
153,503 count from an earlier view/configuration.  Of these, 229 are branch-
only foreground, 1,177 are main-only foreground, and 44,919 are foreground in
both images with different encoded normals.  The MCB pixel is independently
confirmed in main, but the remaining differences are unadjudicated: main is
not automatically an accuracy oracle.

Recovery remains allocation-free during shooting.  It rebuilds a fresh
whole-ray transaction from every ray-visible legacy leaf, accepts only a
complete alternating partition, repeats the solve in the reverse direction,
and requires the reversed ordered distances to agree.  Coarse and refined
roots are never spliced.  An attempted optimization that refined only
certificate-selected cells reduced cost but lost the MCB correction and all
five GTA replay completions; it was rejected and reverted.

All installed and GTA recoveries complete at level 1.  Production therefore
defaults to one replay level, while
`LIBRT_BREP_SELECTIVE_REFINEMENT_MAX_LEVEL=2` retains the former second level
for explicit qualification.  An unresolved level-1 replay simply retains the
complete legacy result.  This does not weaken publication proof and avoids
charging every failed attempt for a 4-by-4 UV grid.  The full-scene aggregate
replay cost fell from 13.064 seconds with both levels to 3.222--4.680 seconds
in current level-1 profiles, with the same five completions, ten hits, zero
reversal mismatches, and identical routed image.

A stronger-looking root-count gate was also measured and rejected as
authority.  Across 397,861 cell/span associations the profile reports 45,982
certified regular roots, 45,882 conservatively counted observations, 97
deficit cells, and 100 deficit roots.  Only 75 of 14,986 replay attempts have
a positive per-cell deficit, and none of the five successful replays do.
Absence of a count deficit is therefore not proof that cleanup has a complete
usable event transaction.  These counters remain profile/audit diagnostics
and do not suppress recovery.

The latest three alternating, unprofiled full-scene samples have routed SHOT
medians of 127.484 CPU / 7.17141 wall seconds and branch-legacy medians of
103.000 / 5.68800 seconds: 23.77 percent CPU and 26.08 percent wall overhead.
Three freshly repeated clean-main samples have medians of 103.266 CPU /
5.50258 wall seconds, putting this loaded-host routed sample 23.45/30.33
percent above main.  This is improved from the preceding 31.6-percent
same-branch CPU result but does not yet satisfy the 20-percent production
ceiling.  Timing varied materially with host load; the final release number
must come from a quiet, alternating archive.  The approximately 68-percent
peak-memory saving remains a legitimate reason to consider a documented
small speed premium, but it is not a reason to misstate the current gate.

Prep remains a separate, solved architecture issue rather than the replay
cost.  In the latest loaded sequence, routed GETTREE median is 18.246 seconds,
branch legacy is 17.327, and clean main is 14.686; the established unloaded
scene-cache checkpoint is approximately 12.9 seconds on the branch versus
10.9 on main.  Routing mode itself adds little prep work.  The old 65--68
second result was the removed per-object eager-pack design.

The permanent `rt_brep_ray_incomplete_transaction_recovery` test injects a
missing endpoint plus unmatched same-side observations and requires forward
and reverse bounded recovery, an empty cleaned legacy transaction, level-1
completion, and no general prepared solve.  The status-2 routing/packless/
throughput tests, positive missing-root audit, and direct-route image gate
remain the focused controls.  Cross-platform sanitizers, broader multi-view
oracle adjudication, and a quiet final performance archive are still required
before enabling the route by default.

The final optimized Ninja rebuild and complete serial focused matrix pass
103/103 tests in 1,407.87 seconds.  This includes the three certificate-
telemetry audits and the status-2 proxy-throughput gate; the latter also
passes a separate isolated rerun.  `regress-repository`, `regress-licenses`,
and `git diff --check` pass on this source checkpoint (the diff check reports
only the expected Windows working-tree line-ending notices).


2026-10-06 EARLIER GTA02 VALIDATION BASELINE (SUPERSEDED WHERE NOTED ABOVE)

The current selective route is locally performance-viable on a complete
top-level assembly, but it is not yet an unconditional production default.
The remaining release work is independent accuracy adjudication, broader
model/platform qualification, sanitizers, and deterministic handling of
overlap claimant/material selection.  There is no known architectural
performance or memory blocker.

The often-quoted GTA02 "68 percent unsupported" population is a routing
classification, not a raytrace failure rate.  In the current profiled
128-square view there are 60,372 B-Rep shot calls.  Of these, 41,060
(68.0117 percent) are calls to open/non-solid B-Reps, for which the certified
solid enter/exit replacement route intentionally has no authority.  Those
calls continue through the legacy intersector and produce normal results.  In
an unprofiled production run the object-level precondition bypasses the full
routing decision for this known population; profiling deliberately evaluates
it so the fallback remains visible.  The additional 4,210 unmatched-root
lookup observations occur inside that same non-solid population.  No
face/leaf identity, ordinal, range, or cache corruption was observed.

The scene-wide cache design is active and depth-safe.  Primitive prep creates
no private cell/span pack allowance.  After scene inventory, an 8 MiB total
budget populates immutable packs for eligible solids and every unpopulated or
partial case retains the allocation-free scalar reconstruction.  The latest
GTA02 prep profile uses 8,208,992 of 8,388,608 bytes and populates 188 of 306
B-Reps.  Persistent prep-cache keys include the selected SurfaceTree depth and
format variant; the serial and concurrent depth-variant, compression,
corruption-recovery, migration, unsupported-cell, and scene-budget tests all
pass.  Direction-coherent entries are prep-owned, immutable when published,
reader-pinned, and keyed by object generation, complete direction, selected
depth, and format.  Ray tracing performs no allocation for grazing
certification.  The 256 by 256 object-direction grid remains the current
choice: its modest retained-memory cost is acceptable within the measured
scene savings, while a future scene-wide direction-cache budget may reduce it
further without changing correctness.

One fail-closed defect found during the final audit is fixed.  A root-workspace
overflow now prevents directional probing, and directional optional work can
no longer overwrite `RT_BREP_ROUTE_QUAL_UNSUPPORTED`.  The permanent
`brep_audit_routing_unsupported_no_work` case proves that an overflowing
transaction launches no optional prepared work and retains legacy authority.
The audit telemetry also names the `directional_grazing` risk lane, and the
publication-fallback and event-class rejection tests now use their distinct
selected pairs (42 and 46 respectively).

An attempted optimization that reconstructed adaptive directional boxes from
an unpacked whole-face control hull was rejected and reverted.  On GTA02 it
increased directional time from approximately 243 to 568 milliseconds and
total routing-decision time from approximately 288 to 618 milliseconds.  The
current packed/cell-local implementation instead records 276.138 milliseconds
of decision time, 232.565 milliseconds of directional work, 29.160
milliseconds of missing-root certification, and two conservative optional
attempts totaling 7.364 milliseconds over 60,372 profiled B-Rep calls.  The
two attempts do not certify, publish nothing, and retain legacy.

The timer-stable 63-face result is close to branch legacy.  Seven alternating
one-CPU 1024-square trials produce 462,820.77 routed versus 489,846.87 legacy
rays/CPU-second and 441,143.48 versus 474,898.55 rays/wall-second.  Routed
therefore retains 94.48 percent of legacy CPU throughput and 92.89 percent of
legacy wall throughput (5.84 and 7.65 percent time overhead respectively).
All fourteen images and their 431,984-call, 195,946-hit, 236,038-miss
aggregates are identical.

The whole-assembly GTA02 result is also within the explicitly accepted
20-percent tracing envelope when measured with material-independent output.
Seven alternating 1024-square, 24-worker surface-normal runs have routed
medians of 107.406 CPU / 4.91244 wall seconds and branch-legacy medians of
98.7656 / 4.48017 seconds.  This is 8.75 percent additional CPU time and 9.65
percent additional wall time.  All fourteen branch images have SHA-256
`C16C4D1160E90F0ABA3282D3CD77D9C293223B33432CE80A585FB124FA47D957`
and exactly 3,833,367 B-Rep calls, 2,265,783 hits, and 1,567,584 misses.  Clean
main's seven-run normal-render medians are 102.188 CPU / 4.77968 wall seconds,
so routed is only 5.11 percent higher in CPU and 2.78 percent higher in wall
time.  Main's repeatable normal hash is
`09D47BBAB48383BC237D85CCD2EA7F2BD2CE962C4C0AD64D0443E10EA52A8C5B`.

The shaded GTA result must not be used alone as a geometry oracle or pure
intersection benchmark.  Coincident assembly overlaps can select different
material claimants after parallel prep; two of three routed 1024-square trials
therefore had a different shaded hash despite identical B-Rep aggregates.
The corresponding normal renders are repeatable and byte-identical between
routed and branch legacy.  At 512 square, where all six shaded runs happened
to use the same claimant ordering, routed medians were 26.2656 CPU / 1.28382
wall seconds versus 22.3438 / 1.02683 for branch legacy: 17.55 percent CPU
and 25.03 percent wall overhead, with the roughly one-second wall interval too
short for a stable wall conclusion.

The branch's peak working set is a major improvement.  Median sampled peaks
from the 1024-square runs are 1,697,243,136 bytes (1.581 GiB) for routed and
5,343,883,264 bytes (4.977 GiB) for clean main, a 68.24-percent reduction.
Current total branch prep is normally about 12.5--13.1 wall seconds versus the
established unloaded clean-main 10.90-second baseline.  In the current
seven-trial 1024-square series, routed total-prep median is 13.08443 seconds
and main is 12.24812 seconds, a 6.83-percent difference; the spread between
these main measurements records host-load sensitivity.  Both results are
within the accepted 20-percent boundary and prep is amortized by larger
renders.  The former 65.9--67.8-second prep result remains closed as duplicated
per-object eager packing, not a fundamental shallow-tree cost.

Accuracy evidence must be stated in two parts.  The permanent routing-truth,
missing-root injection, grazing, seam, endpoint, trim, reversal, and status-2
tests demonstrate controlled cases where independently certified results
recover boundary/contact behavior and safely publish.  The later checkpoint
above additionally confirms one visible GTA MCB correction against main and
five replay completions.  Clean main differs from the current routed normal
image at 46,325 pixels: 229 branch-only foreground, 1,177 main-only
foreground, and 44,919 foreground in both with different normals.  Apart from
the confirmed MCB pixel, those differences cannot yet be labeled improvements;
multi-view rays require an independent high-precision adjudicator.

The optimized Ninja build is clean.  The complete focused expression
`^(rt_brep_|brep_audit_|rt_cache_)` passes 101/101 serial tests in 1,378.91
seconds, including the throughput ratchets.  `git diff --check` passes.  The
measured final `rt.exe`, `librt.dll`, and `brep-audit.exe` SHA-256 values are
`7FB850688EA255BE80D34C62F1596B2DB9DDB5A159C8C7D5E9B559EE99FE2EA2`,

2026-10-05 SCENE-WIDE PACK CACHE AND FINAL PREP DISPOSITION

The alarming GTA02 preparation result was not an inherent cost of shallow
SurfaceTrees, directional grazing certification, or NURBS evaluation.  The
former routing-pack policy applied an 8 MiB allowance independently to every
B-Rep.  It consequently built 193,537 duplicated cell/span packs across 304
of 306 objects, raised aggregate routing preparation from 83.161 to 1,105.031
CPU seconds, and produced 65.854--67.805 seconds of GETTREE wall time.  The
same exact implementation without packs took 12.769--13.131 seconds.  Clean
main takes 10.89963 seconds.

Routing-pack ownership is now scene-wide.  Primitive preparation builds no
packs.  After `rt_prep_parallel` has inventoried all solids, one deterministic
8 MiB default budget is distributed among the scene's B-Reps, capped by each
object's eligible pack bytes, and populated in parallel.  A zero or exhausted
share retains the existing fixed-workspace, allocation-free reconstruction as
the authoritative path.  On GTA02 the scene phase uses 8,029,376 of 8,388,608
bytes and populates 304 of 306 B-Reps.  GETTREE takes 11.97390 seconds and the
separate parallel pack phase takes 0.51297 seconds, for approximately 12.49
seconds total versus 10.90 on main.  Three interleaved routed/legacy samples
have total-prep medians of 12.91986 and 13.02717 seconds respectively, showing
that routing mode itself does not materially change preparation.  The
permanent `rt_brep_scene_pack_cache` test enforces the global byte bound and
nonzero multi-object population.

The directional grazing fixed-stack default is 256 adaptive boxes, selectable
from 1 through 1,024 with
`LIBRT_BREP_DIRECTIONAL_GRAZING_BOX_BUDGET`.  This is ray-visible subdivision,
not uniform SurfaceTree depth, and it performs no ray-time allocation.  The
256-box setting reduces GTA02's former 124 false optional triggers to two,
costing 9.2 aggregate milliseconds; genuine routing-truth corrections remain
covered.  A 1,024-box experiment eliminates those two triggers but does not
recover the remaining 63-face dispatch/certification overhead.

The final one-CPU, 1,024-square, seven-trial 63-face archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-scene8m-budget256-multiface-v1`.  The certified
selective-authority path reaches 444,429.56 rays/CPU-second and 439,992.30
rays/wall-second: 187.4/188.1 percent of clean main and 90.1/90.3 percent of
branch legacy.  Every routed trial repeats and exactly matches branch legacy
in ray count, intersection count, hit/miss aggregates, and result digest.  It
therefore passes both main-relative production speed gates but remains about
ten percent below the same-branch legacy target of 95 percent.  Profiling
attributes the remaining gap to bounded routing decisions, directional
certification, and especially missing-root observation/certification rather
than optional transactions, cache misses, or prep.

The archive manifest/results/summary SHA-256 values are
`5E3A8B7E746A22EE7E958EDD1EC94D181002AE356CC7BFED6AE56DE26DCA1847`/
`EC45F8405F7803D5E9B65A5B2E979C0AF6F5446995503531281689F8A24B753E`/
`39CCB0AFE6CB3126AC6002543719494AC480FB0B1243D4D3EA4A5988ACED4A5A`.
The measured `rt.exe` and `librt.dll` hashes are
`7BBB37996842158FA9254F8BF1969FAB4BAEF7892314B68521BB23752FDA7275`
and
`FDEBB4EA80CA2EF845B50D65ADFBAABC8066E91124E3CB9C415FD59158F8CF6D`.

Post-change targeted verification passes 18/18 tests: the scene-cache bound,
both depth-variant cache tests, status-2 packless/routing/throughput gates,
direct-route and profile-histogram tests, and all ten routing-truth audits.  A
complete optimized Ninja rebuild and `git diff --check` also pass.

This closes prep scaling as a fundamental concern.  Release work still
includes retained/peak scene-memory measurement, the remainder of the focused
matrix, broader cache reload stress, sanitizers, broader STEP/FRAME coverage,
and a larger GTA02 top-level-comb render.  If the 95-percent same-branch target
remains mandatory, the next hot-path task is to reduce missing-root
observation/certificate cost without weakening its fail-closed authority.


2026-10-02 OUTER PROXY/POLYCURVE ROUTING AND FINAL LOCAL VERIFICATION

The next bounded status-2 composition is implemented.  One affine
`ON_CurveProxy` may now select and reverse any proper subdomain of an otherwise
qualified flat `ON_PolyCurve`.  Prep flattens the selected portion into the
existing bounded piece representation and explicitly composes the outer
proxy's orientation/domain map with every leaf arc, line, direct-NURBS, or
single-proxy-piece map.  The complete target polycurve is validated even when
the outer proxy selects only a proper subdomain, so malformed pieces cannot be
hidden outside the queried interval.  Each retained slice still constructs
its revolution surface independently, avoiding whole-polycurve endpoint
averaging.  Nested polycurves, proxy-of-proxy targets, invalid joins,
unsupported pieces, malformed parameter arrays, and capacity overflow remain
fail-closed and whole-ray legacy-authoritative.

The permanent fixtures cover full, reversed, transformed, and proper-
subdomain outer proxies; mixed arc/NURBS/line targets; individually reversed
proxy pieces; and intervals crossing piece boundaries.  Independent locus and
parameter oracles now pass 36,873 proxy samples, 15 proxy boundary intervals,
16,388 polycurve samples, and 15 polycurve boundary intervals.  Seven
malformed or unsupported polycurve forms and two invalid proxy-target rays are
rejected.  Missing-root routing publishes all 16/16 positive rays and rejects
both 2/2 negative rays.  The BRP6 regression serializes the new representation,
reconstructs it against a separate BREP at explicit SurfaceTree depths three
and eight, and requires identical depth, span, feature-cell, capability, and
cell/span-pack populations plus identical routed publication in both probe
directions.

The proxy/polycurve throughput gate now interleaves equal legacy and routed
batches within each of seven trials, eliminating the CPU-state bias seen when
the two implementations were measured in long separate blocks.  Three
stand-alone 1.5-second-window runs retain 96.8--99.1 percent of legacy CPU
throughput and 97.0--99.1 percent of legacy wall throughput with exact output.
The pre-existing direct-proxy gate also passes under the balanced method.

2026-10-02 FLAT POLYCURVE ROUTING AND LOCAL PRODUCTION VERIFICATION

The next bounded status-2 representation slice is implemented.  A valid
`ON_RevSurface` profile may now be a flat `ON_PolyCurve` containing as many as
1,024 individually qualified arc, line, direct-NURBS, or single-proxy pieces.
Prep records each piece's global profile interval, segment and real-curve
domains, orientation, analytic map kind, and effective arc sweep.  Scalar and
interval conversion select that immutable table; intervals are split at every
enclosed piece boundary, checked independently in both directions, and
outward-rounded before their union is associated with prepared spans.  Nested
polycurves, proxy-of-proxy targets, unsupported segment classes, invalid or
nonmonotone domains, material gaps, and over-capacity profiles still fail
closed to the unchanged whole-ray legacy transaction.

The implementation does not rely on `ON_PolyCurve::GetNurbForm` averaging
heterogeneous segment endpoints.  That conversion moved arc/NURBS joins by a
few ulps after ordinary BREP construction and similarity transforms.  Routing
now constructs the prepared revolution surface one qualified profile piece at
a time, assigns every converted piece its global `SegmentDomain`, and sorts
the resulting exact per-piece Bezier spans back into deterministic UV order.
This preserves both one-sided source loci.  Join acceptance is limited to a
4,096-epsilon coordinate-scaled roundoff envelope; a geometrically valid
near-gap outside that envelope is a permanent negative control.

The dense independent oracle passes 16,388 samples and 15 boundary-straddling
intervals over one-, two-, and four-piece arc, line, and mixed arc/NURBS
profiles, including reversal and similarity transforms.  It rejects five
unsupported neighbors: nesting, an unsupported segment class, a material gap,
an OpenNURBS-valid gap outside the routing roundoff envelope, and 1,025-piece
overflow.  End-to-end missing-root tests publish all 10 expected rays in both
directions across one-arc, reversed/transposed two-arc, mixed four-piece,
transformed mixed four-piece, and reversed four-line profiles.  The line case
uses a top-face normal so the transaction proves and publishes one material
interval rather than attempting to substitute a two-partition ray.  The
complete status-2 gate passes unchanged at explicit SurfaceTree depths three
and eight.

The dedicated seven-trial mixed-polycurve workload passes its 95-percent
same-branch gate with exact output.  Three independent invocations retained
98.7--108.7 percent of legacy CPU throughput and 98.8--108.8 percent of legacy
wall throughput.  The depth-aware cache regression now uses the mixed
polycurve torus itself and reconstructs identical routing populations from
fresh and cached preparations at depths three and eight.

The final post-polycurve 63-face archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-status2-polycurve-final-v2`.  Seven alternating,
cache-disabled, one-CPU, 1024-square trials reach 466,033.78 routed
rays/CPU-second and 458,360.62 routed rays/wall-second: 97.2/96.7 percent of
branch legacy and 202.1/202.2 percent of clean main.  Routed and legacy ray
counts, hit counts, and image digests match exactly.  The manifest, results,
and summary SHA-256 values are
`5E5FE907E69F96E72602C8C5C17D54B52FE59E9981B9CEFDB57F537BC7992548`/
`D08B5AF68F05566BB605A871009181344A17BF1C8F7671F8007CFC3146BCB0BE`/
`D27A35233FB756C152F54049AB5F41C7C8129E5D0991DE60AF629C7270141058`.
The corresponding `rt.exe`, `librt.dll`, `rt_brep_ray.exe`, and `rt_cache.exe`
hashes are
`53AF5FE0502B8BEBC54AB67DB515D1CE73C90935772E1E95C270914CEC3624B5`,

The complete optimized Ninja build is clean.  The expanded focused matrix

The redesign is therefore locally production-viable

2026-10-01 SINGLE-LAYER CURVE-PROXY ROUTING AND FINAL VERIFICATION

The next status-2 representation slice is implemented.  A valid direct
`ON_RevSurface` may now use one affine `ON_CurveProxy` layer over an
`ON_ArcCurve`, `ON_LineCurve`, or `ON_NurbsCurve` profile.  Routing composes
`RealCurveParameter`, the already-qualified underlying curve-to-NURBS map,
and `ThisCurveParameter`; proxy reversal and a proper proxy subdomain are
therefore explicit parts of the map rather than inferred from samples.  Arc
subdomains use the trimmed effective angular sweep, matching the span layout
produced by `ON_CurveProxy::GetNurbForm`.  The existing endpoint, midpoint,
round-trip, strict-monotonicity, separability, domain, outward-rounding, and
singular-side checks remain mandatory.  Nested proxies, polycurve targets,
invalid targets, and all unproved forms remain fail-closed and preserve the
whole-ray legacy transaction.

This work also fixes a separate performance defect in SurfaceTree bounding.
OpenNURBS proxy bounding boxes delegate to the complete real curve and ignore
the selected proxy subdomain.  For a trimmed proxy revolution that made every
profile subdivision inherit nearly the full-surface box; depth-eight probe
rays could fill all 128 fixed leaf slots and fall back before routing was
attempted.  `surface_GetBoundingBox` now obtains a conservative bound for the
trimmed proxy revolution from its NURBS form and control hull.  Conversion or
validation failure retains the previous bounding path, so this optimization
does not grant routing authority.

The permanent proxy oracle checks 24,582 dense samples over direct, reversed,
proper-subdomain, and transformed arc proxies; reversed/subdomain line and
NURBS proxies; and an invalid polycurve target.  It reports one required
rejection and maximum normalized point/round-trip errors of 2.50e-15 and
5.82e-16.  End-to-end missing-root routing publishes all 8 expected positive
rays in both directions and rejects both invalid-target rays before optional
publication.  The dedicated seven-trial proxy workload preserves exact
routed/legacy output and reaches 99.90 percent wall and 99.81 percent CPU
throughput.  `rt_brep_ray_status2_proxy_throughput` makes the 95-percent
same-branch gate permanent.

The exact post-proxy 63-face archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-status2-proxy-final-v1`.  Seven alternating,
cache-disabled, one-CPU, 1024-square trials reach 469,292.76 routed
rays/CPU-second and 461,594.88 routed rays/wall-second: 97.2/97.0 percent of
branch legacy and 201.4/202.4 percent of clean main.  Routed and legacy ray
counts, hit counts, and image digests match exactly.  The manifest, results,
and summary SHA-256 values are
`A71A2AFAA0B4E4F004352F3AB0466E979281C2241A09286EC77B4E72AB161468`/

2026-10-01 STATUS-2 ROUTING REDESIGN AND FINAL LOCAL QUALIFICATION

Verdict: the routing redesign is successful for the qualified production path.
There is no observed architectural or performance barrier to bringing the
63-face workload close to its same-branch legacy path.  On the preceding
qualified binary, a 21-trial adjudication reaches 456,522.88 rays/CPU-second and
445,705.26 rays/wall-second: 95.9/95.6 percent of branch legacy and
249.0/252.0 percent of clean main.  The seven-trial imported 214-face route
reaches 90,079.01/84,717.73 rays per CPU/wall second: 98.4/97.2 percent of
branch legacy and 133.4/131.3 percent of main.  Every routed trial repeats and
matches branch-legacy ray count, hit count, and image digest exactly.  These
results demonstrate local production viability behind the existing opt-in
controls; they do not authorize unconditional default promotion before the
external P1, P2, and per-authority-class P4 gates in `brep_next_steps.txt`
pass.

The former status-2 all-spans fallback is removed for one deliberately narrow
class.  A valid direct `ON_RevSurface` whose profile is an `ON_ArcCurve`,
`ON_LineCurve`, or direct `ON_NurbsCurve` now maps each SurfaceTree rectangle
into the prepared NURBS parameter domain.  The mapper requires finite and
in-domain endpoints, forward/inverse round trips, separability at all four
corners, strict one-dimensional monotonicity through the midpoint, and no
incident singular side.  It outward-rounds both mapped endpoints and clamps
only at the known complete NURBS domain.  The mapped interval now controls
cell/span counting, association construction, restricted control packs, and
degree-certificate eligibility.  Polycurves, proxy/other profile types,
nonseparable or noninvertible mappings, collapsed sides, and every failed
check remain explicitly unsupported and whole-ray legacy-authoritative.

The extreme arc case exposed a concrete OpenNURBS numerical failure rather
than a routing-design failure.  At a rational-quadratic quadrant boundary,
`ON_Arc::GetRadianFromNurbFormParameter` could select the antipodal quadratic
root after a one-ULP perturbation when the arc also had a large length-valued
domain, reversal, and surface transposition.  Arc profiles now use the direct
analytic rational-quadratic parameter relation for OpenNURBS' one-, two-, and
four-span representations in both directions.  Analytic inverse checks remain
mandatory, and interval endpoints receive a domain-scaled 4,096-epsilon plus
`nextafter` outward allowance before clipping to the complete NURBS domain.

Permanent end-to-end coverage exercises transformed, scaled, reversed, and
transposed arc-profile and direct-NURBS-profile tori, and a capped line-profile
cylinder with nondefault parameter domains.  All 12 torus routing cases,
including both directions through the combined 1e4 scale, large translation,
reversal, and transposition stress case, obtain the expected narrow
association and certificate behavior.  Both line-profile cases pass.  A new
independent dense oracle checks 20,485 arc/NURBS samples across full, large-
domain, reversed, half, and partial arcs for strict monotonicity, analytic
inverse round trip, and point-locus identity; maximum normalized point and
round-trip errors are 1.78e-15 and 3.47e-16.  The complete status-2 gate passes
unchanged at SurfaceTree depths three and eight.  The unsupported-profile
negative control remains fail closed.  The revolution-sphere pole audit still
records all 1,320 singular cell/span associations as unsupported, proving that
interval mapping does not accidentally grant pole authority.

The representative imported route profile shows the intended structural win.
Mapped filtering reduces correction-cell/span associations from 3,569 to
2,135.  Of those, 2,134 are proved root-free and one determinant case is
incomplete; there are zero false positives, triggered transactions, optional
attempts, or publications.  The final rerun spends 3.881 ms in certificates,
or 1,817.9 ns per association, while rays with the
degree-theorem capability rise from 72,909 to 209,425.  The remaining
incomplete case safely retains legacy and is useful future optimization work,
not a correctness defect.

The generic prep-cache reader also closes a Windows atomic-replacement race.
It previously obtained size from the pathname and then opened the pathname,
so a concurrent writer could replace the object between those operations and
make a valid cache hit look corrupt.  Size and bytes are now obtained through
one open file handle.  A short Windows-only retry is allowed only after the
final pathname has already been observed, so normal cold misses do not wait.
The eight-process/two-depth cache stress failed before this repair because a
second prep rebuilt instead of hitting; afterward it passed 25 consecutive
repetitions.  Cache-format-4 validation, content/version/depth identity,
bounded decompression, atomic self-healing, and distinct depth variants remain
unchanged.

2026-10-01 CACHE FORMAT 4 CORRUPTION RECOVERY AND FINAL LOCAL RECHECK

Verdict: generic-cache corruption recovery is now implemented and P3 in
`brep_next_steps.txt` is locally complete.  Together with the already completed
selective-routing redesign, this leaves no observed local correctness, cache,
memory, or throughput barrier to production use behind the opt-in controls.
It does not waive the Linux sanitizer/thread/leak, second-platform, unavailable-
corpus, or unsupported-authority gates, so unconditional default promotion
remains blocked by P1, P2, and per-class P4 qualification.  This Windows host
reports that WSL is not installed, so it cannot supply the required Linux
sanitizer or independent-platform evidence.

The generic prep-cache format is bumped from 3 to 4.  Before the historical DB5
pointer parser sees a cache object, a bounded parser now verifies the exact
outer object length, required flags and types, every variable-width field,
terminators, and zero padding.  The embedded object name must match its
content-derived pathname.  Attributes are bounded and structurally validated
before import; MIME type and the complete prep version must match.  Each format-
4 entry stores a 128-bit fingerprint of the complete compressed body.  Load
requires that fingerprint before a bounded LZ4 decoder receives the body; the
decoder knows both input and output lengths and must consume the stream while
producing exactly the declared byte count.  Invalid sizes, malformed streams,
and semantic deserialization failures all release owned memory and fall back to
normal primitive prep.

Publication remains a temporary-file transaction followed by atomic rename.
It now deliberately replaces an existing same-name entry after fresh prep, so
a rejected but readable file cannot permanently poison that content key.  All
valid concurrent writers for a key produce equivalent content.  Formats 1--3
are deterministically cleared and reinitialized as format 4; the cache identity
still includes primitive serialization/configuration, including BREP depth.

Two permanent cache integrations cover the new contract.  Test 12 injects a
truncated DB5 object, a UINT32_MAX declared output size, a damaged LZ4 stream
whose fingerprint is recomputed, malformed attributes, a wrong prep version, a
wrong embedded name, and a body/fingerprint mismatch.  For every case it
requires a noncached rebuild, same-path atomic replacement, and then an exact
zero-tree-build cache hit.  Test 13 converts a populated format marker from 4
to 3 and requires deterministic 3-to-4 invalidation, fresh reconstruction, and
a subsequent hit.  The existing depth-variant and eight-writer race tests still
pass.

The exact-source `brlcad_build/brep-routing-suite-nist-v9` archive passes 8
installed NIST databases, 11 BREP solids, 1,298 rays, 22 fresh preparations,
and 44 isolated cold/warm cache preparations with zero exits, prep/cache
failures, prepared/legacy differences, depth differences, reversal mismatches,
lookup failures, or missed corrections.  All fresh/cold/warm populations agree.
For the three depth-eight cases at the 1,048,575-subpatch cap, NIST 2 records
fresh/cold/warm times of 3.020629/3.453865/2.997272 seconds and warm RSS 9.2

2026-09-30 FINAL CACHE-LIFETIME AND PRODUCTION-VIABILITY VERIFICATION

Verdict: the selective routing redesign is implemented and locally demonstrated
as a production-viable NURBS raytracer.  There is no observed architectural or
performance barrier on the qualified 63-face and 214-face paths.  It remains an
opt-in, fail-closed route rather than the unconditional default: unsupported
authority classes retain legacy, and the external sanitizer, platform, and
unavailable-corpus release gates listed in `brep_next_steps.txt` are not waived.

The cache lifetime redesign removes the process-lifetime mapped-file/hash entry
store.  Every cache lookup now reads an owned transient buffer, releases the
compressed cache object immediately after decompression, and releases the
decoded BRP6 external after SurfaceTree deserialization but before rebuilding
the much larger derived routing store.  The deserializer ownership contract is
documented in `include/rt/func.h` and `include/rt/functab.h`; callers may always
call `bu_free_external` after deserialization, including when the primitive has
already consumed it.  Compression failure, serialization failure, temporary-
file open failure, malformed size, decompression failure, and rejected-attribute
paths now release their owned allocations.  The generic cache identity still
includes the primitive's queried format/configuration variant, so BRP6 depth
three and depth eight coexist without aliasing.  Atomic temporary publication
and the eight-writer, two-depth race test are preserved.

Certificate preparation received two exact work reductions.  The fixed
1,048,575-subpatch limit is checked before constructing intervals and
subpatches, so associations beyond the cap do no discarded exact work.  Each
hierarchy node now performs one midpoint de Casteljau split and retains both
children instead of independently restricting the same parent twice.  This
does not change interval semantics or any retained routing population.  A BRP7
format that serializes subpatch boxes was considered but is not currently
justified: the lifetime and construction fixes make warm BRP6 load faster than
fresh prep on the largest cases without the larger payload and compatibility
risk.

The final `brlcad_build/brep-routing-suite-nist-v7` archive passes 8 installed
NIST databases, 11 BREP solids, 1,298 rays, 22 fresh depth-three/depth-eight
preps, and 44 isolated cold/warm cache preps.  It reports zero object exits,
prep or cache-prep failures, prepared/legacy differences, depth differences,
reversal mismatches, feature-cell lookup failures, or missed corrections.  The
728 unmapped roots remain only the expected fail-closed observations beyond the
fixed leaf workspace.  All fresh/cold/warm tree, cell, unsupported-cell, pack,
control-interval, and subpatch populations agree exactly.

Relative to the pre-redesign NIST v6 archive, the worst cold prep falls from
6.251846 to 3.536414 seconds, the worst warm prep from 5.940228 to 2.972934
seconds, and the worst warm peak RSS from 607,289,344 to 431,259,648 bytes.
For NIST 2 at depth eight, fresh/cold/warm are 3.281832/3.536414/2.972934
seconds with 370.1/532.7/411.3 MiB peaks; its warm time improves 45.2 percent
and warm peak improves 29.0 percent from v6.  NIST 4 records
2.839158/3.324032/2.731938 seconds with 362.6/503.4/393.8 MiB peaks; its fresh
and warm times improve 52.0 and 54.0 percent.  Maximum exact retained storage
remains 329,511,548 bytes.  Raw/compressed payload totals remain
527,511,971/215,835,513 bytes, confirming that the improvement comes from work
and lifetime changes rather than an incompatible cache representation.

2026-09-30 FINAL ASSOCIATION PACKING, NIST V5, AND PERFORMANCE GATE

Verdict: the selective routing redesign remains production-viable after the
cache/resource work, and its local correctness, cache, speed, and source-policy
gates are green.  It is not yet justified as the unconditional default: the
unavailable sanitizer/platform and broader-corpus gates, plus unsupported
authority classes, remain explicit release blockers and continue to fail
closed to legacy.

The generic prep cache had a real compression-size defect.  LZ4 output was
allocated with `LZ4_compressBound`, but the cache external's byte count was set
to that maximum rather than to the actual byte count returned by LZ4.  The new
internal `_rt_cache_compress_external` helper records exactly the returned
payload plus its four-byte uncompressed-size header and frees its temporary
buffer on failure.  The permanent `rt_cache_payload_compression` regression
compresses a one-MiB payload and requires the stored result to be smaller.
Together with the depth-qualified cache UUID and BRP6 payload checks, this
closes the known depth/cache correctness issue without changing the on-disk
BREP format.

`brep-audit --prep-only` now distinguishes raw BRP6 bytes, actual compressed
bytes, retained used bytes, unused vector/arena capacity, and retained core,
ON_Brep, SurfaceTree, CurveTree, edge, surface, hierarchy, routing, and vertex
categories.  On depth-eight NIST 2 `Document_item.s`, the 125,056,675-byte raw
payload compresses to 57,795,784 bytes, a 53.8-percent reduction, in about
0.16 seconds.  Before subpatch packing, exact accounted retention was
368,590,004 bytes, of which routing data was 207,454,360 bytes; 356,103,004
bytes were used and 12,487,000 were spare container/arena capacity.  This made
the derived certificate store, not serialized BRP6, the leading live-memory
target.

Each of the 1,048,575 retained certificate subpatch records carried a double
coordinate scale and validity flag that traversal never read: all subpatches
use their enclosing pack's single conservative expansion.  Those cold fields
are removed and a compile-time invariant requires the record to remain exactly
one `ON_BoundingBox` (48 rather than 64 bytes on this build).  The subpatch work
cap is now an explicit 2^20 records independent of C++ layout; this is important
because continuing to divide a byte cap by `sizeof(record)` silently admitted
33 percent more certificate work and consumed the saved memory in additional
control intervals.  The corrected cap preserves the exact pre-change
1,048,575-box, 637,586-control, and 146,657-pack populations.

The subsequent association-layout pass removes the redundant parallel
feature-cell/span-index vector and makes each cell-span pack's `span_index` the
single binding authority.  Pack indices and counts are now checked `uint32_t`
values and orders are checked `uint8_t` values.  Feature-cell association
offsets/counts are also `uint32_t`.  A read-only SurfaceTree pass counts exactly
the associations that construction will emit and reserves the pack vector once,
so both binding and pack unused capacity are zero without a build-then-copy
peak.  Finally, feature cells no longer retain an unused coordinate scale and
validity flag; the correction BVH and each certificate pack retain the only
scales consulted by traversal.  The count pass was checked predicate by
predicate against construction: invalid/degenerate placeholder leaves have no
associations, correction cells use the same trim/domain-boundary test, status-1
faces use the same interval-overlap filter, status-2 faces retain every face
span, and both paths reject mismatched ranges, faces, and 32-bit overflow.

After all packing, depth-eight NIST 2 `Document_item.s` retains exactly
329,511,548 bytes, of which 168,375,928 are routing data.  Relative to the
original attributed 368,590,004-byte layout, this removes 39,078,456 bytes
(10.60 percent); routing retention falls by 39,078,432 bytes.  Used storage is
324,332,252 bytes and unused capacity is 5,179,296 bytes.  Routing accounts for
4,051,040 unused bytes: feature cells, the removed binding vector, and packs are
all zero; controls account for 3,988,928 and subpatches for 62,112.  The unchanged
populations are 380,302 leaves/cells, 146,657 packs, 637,586 control intervals,
and 1,048,575 subpatch boxes.  Three fresh processes report the invariant
retained count, median peak RSS 395,554,816 bytes, and median prep time 5.295403
seconds.  Against the original 427,200,512-byte representative peak this is a
31.65 MB (7.41-percent) observed reduction.  Raw/compressed BRP6 sizes remain
125,056,675/57,795,784 bytes because associations and packs are rebuilt after
load rather than serialized.

The final-layout `brlcad_build/brep-routing-suite-nist-v5` archive passes all
8 installed databases, 11 BREP solids, 1,298 rays, and 22 fresh depth-three/
depth-eight prep runs.  It reports zero object exits, prep failures,
prepared/legacy differences, depth differences, reversal mismatches, feature-
cell lookup failures, or missed corrections.  Across the suite, 527,511,971
raw cache bytes compress to 215,835,513 bytes; maximum retained preparation is
329,511,548 bytes and maximum process peak RSS is 395,796,480 bytes.  The
manifest/database CSV/resource CSV/resource JSONL/routing JSONL SHA-256 values
are respectively
`F9AAD874DF073E0A72F5FEE3713BFDB1F81F0BD6954D85E6D814AD66EE2ABB4F`,
`4BB568D2A67BE518A29F148FC191AE9B5CDE62498E0A95D0BB87C310278A9990`,
`54F6D78445A241FD3A92B202762F723602D8BFDB2F02B41904AEC9A3D5FA8BE0`,
`C40C74C1FECF283ED6F9625A61203C44B90DD0AA94D0F5D643CB3B946B032AA6`,
and `47F697DE8F1F5EA8FAABD7FE4C4A64C93BFE23395728D65627FFD4B113248802`.

Final-layout, cache-disabled, one-CPU, 1024-square seven-trial archives reconfirm

The complete optimized Ninja build succeeds.  The expanded focused matrix

2026-09-30 ROOTED NONCONTACT FAST REJECTION AND STABLE NEAR-LEGACY GATE

missing-root certificate, contact streams still run the complete classifier,
and every unsupported or incomplete case retains legacy.  Profiling and the
