2026-09-30 IMPORTED PREP/CACHE QUALIFICATION AND FINAL PERFORMANCE RECHECK

Verdict: the selective routing redesign is successfully implemented and is
already substantially faster than clean main on both representative renders.
It is viable for production use behind the existing opt-in controls.  It is
not yet ready to replace legacy as the unconditional default because external
sanitizer/platform gates, broader imported-corpus coverage, unsupported event
classes, and a small but repeatable 63-face near-legacy optimization gap remain.
This section supersedes the earlier same-date test counts and performance-gate
status below; those sections remain as implementation history.

The installed imported corpus exposed a real prep defect after the routing
redesign: six valid NIST BREP solids could construct their legacy SurfaceTrees
but failed primitive prep when optional routing-cell construction encountered
degenerate leaves with invalid boxes or non-increasing parameter domains.
Those leaves now receive stable, serializable `UNSUPPORTED` routing
placeholders.  They retain legacy authority, cannot enter the missing-root
certificate BVH, and cannot make optional routing metadata a prerequisite for
legacy BREP prep.  Any other optional routing-index construction failure also
disables that metadata and fails closed instead of failing the primitive.

The versioned `brep-routing-suite-nist-v3` archive exercises all installed
NIST 1--11 databases at candidate depth three and reference depth eight.  It
passes 8 databases, 11 BREP solids, 1,298 rays, and 22 independent fresh prep
runs with zero object exits, resource-prep failures, prepared/legacy
differences, depth differences, prepared or legacy reversal mismatches,
feature-cell lookup failures, or missed corrections.  It records 728 roots
omitted only after the expected fixed leaf-workspace overflow.  Six solids
exercise unsupported placeholders: 1,586 cells at depth three and 54,946 at
depth eight.  This converts the discovered imported-prep failure into measured
fail-closed coverage rather than silently excluding the affected geometry.

Fresh resource probes now report current/peak RSS, prep time, exact raw BRP6
payload bytes, cache variant, SurfaceTree populations, routing-cell
populations, and derived certificate-store populations.  The largest observed
depth-eight raw payload is 125,056,675 bytes (about 119.3 MiB) on NIST 2; the
largest process peak is 427,003,904 bytes (about 407.2 MiB).  NIST 2 depth eight
contains 380,302 leaves, 146,657 cell/span packs, and 1,048,575 cached subpatch
boxes and prepares in 5.31 seconds.  NIST 4 reaches about 106.3 MiB raw payload,
about 394 MiB peak RSS, and 5.91 seconds.  Depth three has no observed ray
difference in this corpus and is much smaller, but this evidence does not yet
justify changing the global production depth.

The permanent `rt_cache_brep_unsupported_cells` regression uses NIST 11, whose
depth-three tree contains 307 invalid-box placeholder leaves.  It performs
cold writes at depths three and eight, requires two distinct cache objects,
then reloads both variants in fresh `rt_i` instances.  A reload must report
zero SurfaceTree build time and exactly reproduce every tree, packed-control,
routing-risk, unsupported-cell, binding, certificate-pack, and correction-BVH
count.  This directly qualifies BRP6 serialization/deserialization of the
invalid-box placeholders as well as depth-key separation; the synthetic
concurrent depth test remains the publication-race gate.

The final combined seven-trial, cache-disabled, one-CPU 1024-square archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-production-final-v2`.  On the 214-face imported
`Solid1_3.s`, selective publication reaches 148,800.14 median rays/CPU-second,
98.0 percent of branch legacy and 144.6 percent of clean main.  On the 63-face
fixture it reaches 466,033.78, 93.7 percent of branch legacy and 195.1 percent
of clean main.  A separate seven-trial repeat in
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-production-final-v3` records 453,438.27, 93.9
percent of branch legacy and 189.9 percent of main.  Every routed trial repeats
exactly and matches the branch-legacy ray/hit aggregate and image digest.
The v2 manifest/results/summary SHA-256 values are
`04CD1E93D7F004923C4CB1262E17279A2B12DCFCF00B14A038BABEE69F7E4FF4`,
`1933B55001CB96997CF6356E022D99F3C7A17BEF6AFA79939D1BB117A573823A`,
and `4C3FB61D928B1EF54DD22450B7A14402EBACEDA1F9ADA376AD441B4B529A1C4F`.
The corresponding v3 values are
`ABA24C158BBD24E2A00FD54D8805DB579524A70EB0D66E9057A64FF5591107AA`,
`BAB5A9B4885671FEEA42A9DEAE237C80AF4850DCF14DBD8271C698BD4E87E357`,
and `9DCFFC6FC4AFCA69A96C79B246CC190122B9FA98F28D662CC38D12227FAB1556`.

Thus the answer to the original main-comparability question is firmly yes:
the 63-face route remains roughly 1.9--2.0 times as fast as clean main, and no
fundamental NURBS, topology, cache, or routing barrier is evident.  The stronger
internal target of staying within five percent of the optimized branch-legacy
path is not yet stable: earlier archives measured 95.1--97.9 percent, while the
two final-source archives measure 93.7--93.9 percent.  Current profiling assigns
about 128.6 ms over 431,984 BREP shots to route decisions, including about
45.9 ms for 65,735 certificate associations.  This is a bounded optimization
target, not an architectural blocker; default promotion should wait for a
repeatable margin or an explicitly approved exception.

The complete optimized Ninja build succeeds.  After rebuilding all consumers
of the private prep-statistics structure, the focused
`^(rt_brep_|brep_audit_|rt_cache_)` matrix passes 90/90 in 46.95 seconds:
38 `rt_brep_*`, 42 `brep_audit_*`, and 10 `rt_cache_*` tests.  The NIST v3
suite and both final performance archives were produced from the rebuilt
binary.  Linux ASan/UBSan, TSan, leak, and at least one additional-platform
run remain external gates because this MSVC host cannot execute them.  The
unavailable FRAME, Recurta, SC, UCP, and 0353 corpora and natural positives for
status-2, seam, pole, singular, tangent, and mixed-hazard authority also remain
explicit release gates; unsupported cases continue to retain legacy.


2026-09-30 BOUNDED CERTIFICATE, FLEXIBLE CACHE, AND ROUTING CLOSURE

Verdict: the redesigned route is implemented and production-viable, but it is
not yet justified as the default authority for every BREP.  On the 63-face
production-shaped render, the complete opt-in missing-root certificate route
reaches 469,292.76 median rays/CPU-second, 95.1 percent of the 493,447.53
branch legacy-only control and 197.9 percent of the 237,133.80 clean-main
control.  Routed and branch-legacy ray/hit aggregates and image digests match
exactly in every one of seven trials.  This closes the architectural and
63-face speed question: no fundamental performance barrier has been observed.

The production-shaped missing-root route is now implemented behind
`LIBRT_BREP_ROUTE_MISSING_ROOT_CERTIFICATE=1`.  It runs only with the existing
opt-in direct router; publication still requires the independent
`LIBRT_BREP_ROUTE_PUBLISH=1` switch, and legacy remains the default authority.
For an empty legacy root stream, intersected correction-risk SurfaceTree cells
select their bound prepared spans.  Each supported status-1 cell/span
association has a bounded, fail-closed certificate that uses cached outward
homogeneous-control intervals, a small cached subpatch-box hierarchy, hull
exclusion, bounded subdivision, and the existing degree theorem.  It returns
only `root-free`, an exact positive regular-root count, or `incomplete` with an
explicit reason.  Status-2 mappings, unresolved boundary/tangency cases,
nonfinite input, and resource exhaustion remain incomplete rather than being
treated as absence.

A complete root-free result requests no optional work.  A complete positive
result requests the general prepared whole-ray transaction rather than the
boundary/contact-only empty-partition transaction.  The prepared solver must
still establish the material partition; only a complete supported result may
be published, and publication remains an atomic replacement of the untouched
legacy transaction.  Any incomplete or unsupported certificate or prepared
solve falls through to whole-ray legacy.  Positive certificate counts are
surface-root evidence, not by themselves publication authority.

The cache design is now both depth-aware and extensible.  The generic cache key
contains the primitive's complete queried format/configuration variant, and
BRP6 preserves the requested SurfaceTree depth and immutable routing cells.
Cell/span bindings, certificate packs, and the correction-cell BVH are derived
and validated on fresh prep and cache reload, so depth-three and depth-eight
objects cannot alias.  The pack caches only ray-independent data and is bounded
independently to 64 MiB of restricted control intervals and 64 MiB of subpatch
boxes; a pack that cannot be constructed remains explicitly incomplete.
Ray/line answers are never cached.  Cached subpatch-box rejection expands its
boxes outward using the active model tolerance and floating-point scale.

The latest full 97-object, depth-eight archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-routing-corpus-cellpack-tol-v2`.
Its 16,828 rays retain all 18 established corrections with zero missed
corrections, depth differences, cell-lookup failures, prepared reversals, or
legacy reversals.  The one nonzero object is the already known non-solid
`box_minus_cyl_shared_vertices.s`.  Real empty-stream certificate work contains
868 associations: 844 root-free, 24 fail-closed status-2 cases, and zero
positive associations.  Counterfactual removal of known roots exercises
39,238 associations and produces 13,878 positive associations.  Certificate
accounting is exact in both populations.  This is strong negative and
mechanism evidence.  The natural corpus still contains no
legacy-missing/prepared-hit production example, but the permanent
fault-injection gate described below now supplies an independently checked
transaction-level positive.

The final balanced-order seven-trial benchmark archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-certificate-positive-gate-v2`.
Routed and branch-legacy medians are 469,292.76 and 493,447.53
rays/CPU-second respectively, so routing passes the stated >=95-percent
near-legacy throughput gate at 95.1 percent.  Clean main reaches 237,133.80,
making the complete route 197.9 percent of main.  Two timing outliers widen
the raw routed and baseline ranges, but do not move the medians;
the preceding independent seven-trial archive has the same routed and legacy
medians.  The benchmark now alternates legacy/routed order as well as
branch/main order to avoid systematic thermal bias.  A full-size instrumented
profile records 431,984 BREP shots, 129.912 ms of route-decision work, and
46.140 ms inside the certificate.  Its 65,735 associations are all root-free:
53,674 cached-box exclusions, 3,282 direct hull exclusions, and 4,704 complete
subdivision exclusions; all 4,075 degree attempts complete.  The result is
near the deliberately aggressive branch-legacy threshold, so representative
imported-scene timing was measured separately rather than assumed.

The positive transaction is now exercised by a deterministic private
geometric fault injection, not merely by hiding roots at the routing-decision
boundary.  It removes the collected raw legacy hits before cleanup forms the
retained base transaction, while preserving the intersected feature cells and
independently prepared transaction.  Across 94 rays it suppresses 152 raw hits
and 158 route-root observations on 40 rays; 38 rays visibly change from their
normal legacy result.  Sixteen certificate decisions are positive, including
12 exposed faults.  Ten general prepared passes complete and publish.  Four
exposed positives have exact independent prepared/normal-legacy oracle
agreement and all four are recovered; all ten publications match the prepared
transaction, while all 84 nonpublications reproduce the complete injected base
transaction.  There are zero decision or transaction mismatches.  This closes
the independently constructed missing-root fault, general-solver selection,
and atomic-fallback gate.  A naturally occurring legacy miss is still useful
corpus coverage, but is no longer the only way to qualify these mechanics.

The first production-style imported timing is also complete.  The STEP-derived
NIST `Solid1_3.s` fixture has 214 faces and about 129,589 depth-eight
SurfaceTree leaves.  The seven-trial 1024-square archive
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-imported-nist-v1`
measures routed legacy-authority at 148,143.19 rays/CPU-second, branch legacy
at 149,130.81, and clean main at 101,989.16: 99.3 percent of branch legacy and
145.3 percent of main.  Every routed aggregate and digest matches branch
legacy.  A separate publication-enabled one-trial archive,

associations (1,841 root-free and 1,728 fail-closed status-2), zero positives,
zero optional attempts, zero lookup failures/overflows, 94.387 ms of route

The full optimized Ninja build succeeds at this source checkpoint.  The
focused `^(rt_brep_|brep_audit_|rt_cache_)` matrix passes 89/89 in 43.74
seconds, including the stronger positive fault transaction and the concurrent
BRP6 cache stress.  The latter races eight processes across cold depth-three
and depth-eight publication, immediately reloads each variant, requires exactly
two complete cache objects, and passed ten consecutive repeat-until-fail runs.
The stronger positive fault transaction also passes ten consecutive runs.
The final `regress-repository` and `regress-licenses` checks pass.  Remaining
default-authority gates are sanitizer and cross-platform runs plus wider
imported-corpus and unobserved correction-class coverage.  Status-2 maps,

2026-09-30 DEPTH-AWARE FEATURE-CELL ROUTING REDESIGN

Verdict: the routing redesign is viable, and the 63-face case has no observed
fundamental performance blocker.  The production-shaped router now reaches
479,349.03 median rays/CPU-second, 97.86 percent of the 489,845.72 branch
legacy-only control and 202.85 percent of the 236,298.82 clean-main control.
All seven routed trials reproduce the branch-legacy ray/hit aggregates and
image digest exactly.  The remaining work is a bounded cell-local geometric
certificate and broader qualification, not another routing architecture.

The decisive correction to the previous analysis is that the 14 supposedly
missed hidden-root controls did not establish a whole-ray recall requirement.
They are seven pairs of clean ordinary roots on `2cyl_04.s`, `cone_002.s`,
`cone_003.s`, `cone_007.s`, `cone_008.s`, and `cyl_09.s`, with trim distances
from 1.89 through 9.97.  A whole-face scan found them only because the same ray
crossed an unrelated correction-risk cell elsewhere.  They are not roots of
that cell and cannot be valid positive controls for a correction-cell route.
Production therefore does not need a whole-face scan to preserve their recall.

Each depth-specific SurfaceTree leaf now has immutable routing data: its 3-D
box and coordinate scale, face and leaf identity, parameter domain,
capability/risk bits, and a flat range of prepared-span indices.  Status-1
faces bind only parameter-overlapping spans.  Because the parameter map of a
status-2 conversion is not yet certified, each status-2 correction cell binds
conservatively to every prepared span on its face.  A compact BVH over only
correction-risk cells supports an allocation-free, root-independent audit.
The normal direct path incurs no second traversal: it reuses the SurfaceTree
cells already intersected before legacy root solving and follows their
immutable span bindings.

The production-shaped first-stage signal is now spatial intersection with a
correction-risk cell.  The prior full-span Bernstein hull rejected too little
to justify repeating coefficient work that the future cell-local certificate
must perform anyway, so full-span and restricted hull work remains audit-only.
On the focused `multi_cylinder_01.s` audit, real empty-stream whole-face
candidates fell from 102 faces and 92 spans to 16 faces and 10 spans, and broad
signals fell from 46 rays to four, while all eight completed true-root controls
remain signaled.  The independent correction-cell BVH remains in audit mode to
detect accidental dependence on the legacy root ledger.

The latest cache-disabled, one-CPU, 1024-square seven-trial archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-raytrace-risk-cell-bvh-v3`.
Routed throughput is 479,349.03 rays/CPU-second (range
469,292.76--489,845.72), compared with branch legacy-only at 489,845.72
(479,349.03--500,812.42) and clean main at 236,298.82
(231,409.88--240,533.56).  That is a 2.19-percent CPU-time increment over
branch legacy and more than twice main's throughput.  The manifest, results,
and summary SHA-256 values are respectively
`dc4bd207a714e97a91e0621bb9cd3b26b67b5df126ff324c37167fc5c6465203`,
`5299bfa11f61b3d0e4faa518c654841ec501089e2319d1857bec20bab9b87157`,
and `8e8485aca95afe4973ddd7e9c0b629d9257b0259e8a4c8a521dc8b48f5e1d067`.
A post-build production profile records 76.409 ms of routing decision time over
431,984 BREP shots (0.177 microseconds/shot), versus 73.090 ms and 0.169
microseconds/shot with the probe disabled: approximately 3.3 ms total added
decision work for the render.  It records 36,953 signal rays, 65,735 cell/span
associations, and no workspace, lookup, or candidate failure.

The principal remaining performance risk is the cost of the cell-local
certificate, not the cell lookup itself.  This render has
36,953 signaled empty rays and 65,735 cell/span associations.  From the median
2.140625-second legacy-only and 2.187500-second routed shot times, the strict
five-percent-over-legacy goal leaves about 60 ms total, or 1.63 microseconds
per signaled ray if every signal reaches the certificate.  That is an
aggressive target for interval isolation and trim classification and may
require a cheaper certified rejection, precomputed bounds, or batching.  It is
not a main-speed blocker: matching 95 percent of clean main leaves roughly
67 microseconds per signal on this fixture.  Therefore main comparability is
already secure; near-legacy performance after adding missing-root authority is
the remaining optimization risk and must be measured rather than assumed.
There is one unavoidable information bound: if legacy emits no root, a router
cannot distinguish a true miss from an empty cell without doing some
independent geometric proof.  The redesign succeeds by confining that proof to
preselected cells and making every incomplete result retain legacy; it cannot
eliminate the proof altogether.

The generic prep-cache name now includes the primitive's queried complete
format/configuration variant rather than assuming one payload per transformed
object.  BRP6 uses that mechanism to remain depth-specific.  It serializes the
depth-specific SurfaceTree cell identity and domains; cache reload reconstructs
and validates the derived cell-to-span ranges and correction-cell BVH against
those cells.
Consequently depth three and depth eight retain distinct cache identities and
the new derived routing indices cannot be reused at the wrong depth.  No
redundant derived-BVH payload or cache-format increment was necessary.

The refreshed depth-eight 97-object archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-routing-corpus-risk-cell-bvh-v3`.
Its 16,828 rays reproduce all 18 established corrections in nine pairs with
zero missed corrections, unresolved correction oracles, depth differences,
reversal mismatches, cell-lookup failures, or overflow-only unmapped roots.
For real empty streams the cell-local audit signals 574 rays, all 535 completed
independent results are empty, and 39 remain unresolved; the whole-face oracle
signals 626 rays, all 579 completed results are empty, and 47 remain
unresolved.  Thus the broader scan adds false-positive traffic and still finds
no genuine prepared-hit/legacy-miss case.  In the counterfactual population,
the cell route signals 2,446 of 2,460 whole-ray root controls; the whole-face
oracle signals all 2,460 and recovers exactly the 14 ordinary roots already
shown not to belong to the selected correction cell.  This corroborates the
cell-local interpretation rather than a whole-face production requirement.
The manifest, summary, JSONL, cleanup CSV, and capacity CSV SHA-256 values are
respectively
`065ba90dde7aed552dc496cc4417468b6a49da2917214b5ad53ccb36fb2f5a97`,
`8d92a20d222726dd0c26400bde80a4449839f9d9708a8e618f20127a0e4e6f14`,
`ba67ab966e5b8798f72f7d10fddc43d3c7ec16033085d0fa8475377027997716`,
`b011a67530adca32fdc95b79236bffc38bddb0b6362acc05aea4fde2c815d3c4`,
and `8540a680ad35a30083076031a47bb08d830bff97511bba5df1853798689456c2`.

The complete optimized Ninja build succeeds.  The resulting binary passes the
focused `^(rt_brep_|brep_audit_|rt_cache_)` matrix: 87/87 tests in 40.31
seconds at four-way concurrency, including the two revised missing-root
population gates.  The BRP6 depth-variant test now also compares reconstructed
cell-to-span binding and correction-BVH node counts between fresh and cached
preps.  `regress-repository` and `regress-licenses` also pass.  Missing-root
publication is still disabled.
Before extending authority, implement a bounded trim-aware cell-local
root-existence/classification certificate, compare its certified roots with
the retained legacy ledger, and request the existing atomic prepared
transaction only for a complete material difference.  Keep status-2 cells
conservative until their parameter maps are independently certified.  Then
qualify the extension on genuine/injected positives, matched negatives, the
97-object corpus, representative imported renders, and sanitizers.


2026-09-30 ROOT-INDEPENDENT ROUTING PROBE AND REDESIGN DECISION

Verdict: the orthogonal, fail-closed routing redesign is successful for the
currently certified boundary/contact class and is a viable architecture for
additional classes.  There is no fundamental 63-face performance, NURBS,
topology, depth-cache, or publication-transaction blocker.  The remaining
missing-root problem does impose one unavoidable information constraint: a
router that examines only roots produced by legacy cannot discover a root
that legacy omitted.  Additional authority for that class therefore requires
an independent prepared-data query.  The new query is cheap enough to fit the
render budget, but its first conservative signal is not yet selective or
complete enough to launch optional work.  Missing-root publication remains
disabled.

An allocation-free shadow probe now runs automatically in explicit audit
traces.  On a supported empty legacy stream it combines the ray with immutable
prepared homogeneous span controls reached through correction-risk feature
cells.  An outward Bernstein hull rejects a span only when one projected
ray/surface equation cannot be zero.  The full prepared-span hull is the
conservative per-span signal.  A cell-restricted hull is recorded only as
diagnostic telemetry because feature-cell and prepared-span parameter maps
have not yet been independently certified; the restricted result already
misses a known boundary control.  The probe never changes optional work,
publication authority, or output.  Production-shaped cost and population
measurement require `LIBRT_BREP_ROUTE_MISSING_ROOT_PROBE=1` in addition to
direct routing.

Audit traces also make a counterfactual second decision that hides existing
boundary/contact roots.  This supplies stress controls without pretending
that an observed root is a genuine legacy miss.  The permanent
`brep_audit_missing_root_probe` fixture has eight independently completed
true-root controls, all eight signaled; six independently completed empty
controls, four signaled and two rejected; 22 unresolved controls; and no
coefficient failure or workspace overflow.  The direct production regression
requires probe signals while proving zero optional attempts/publications and
an output byte-identical to legacy.

The complete depth-eight 97-object archive is
`brlcad_build/brep-routing-corpus-missing-root-probe-v1`.
Ninety-six solids contribute 16,828 rays; the remaining listed object is the
previously known non-solid.  The run reproduces all 18 established
prepared-miss/legacy-hit corrections in nine pairs, with zero missed
corrections, unresolved correction oracles, depth differences, reversal
mismatches, feature-cell lookup failures, verifier attempts, or probe
workspace/coefficient failures.  It contains no genuine
prepared-hit/legacy-miss correction.  On real empty legacy streams the probe
signals 574 rays: independent prepared work proves 535 empty, proves zero
hits, and leaves 39 unresolved.  Consequently the current signal has no
positive missing-root example and would send mostly known-empty traffic to a
second stage.

The broader counterfactual population reaches the same conclusion.  Of 2,460
independently completed true-root controls, 2,446 signal and 14 do not.  Of
189 independently completed empty controls, 177 signal and only 12 reject;
4,523 controls remain unresolved.  The full-span hull retains 22,005 of 23,422
candidate spans, while the diagnostic restriction retains 21,571 of those
22,005.  Thus this probe is useful as a cheap safety screen, but it fails both
the required 100-percent recall and useful false-positive-work gates for
authority.  The archive manifest, summary, JSONL, cleanup CSV, and capacity
CSV SHA-256 values are respectively
`b51e5e939398bf569c0434995229cf4516a5731aa22a50c49369601ee73b524c`,
`fd1b995ccf8a4f158fed1dd0276fcd2d6bf55f3159ef63e92c2cc02822af24a5`,
`27cd8d977ca07e63602ef294cbe9b6a413f951d3b343dc6bf603c935244e0ee4`,
`b011a67530adca32fdc95b79236bffc38bddb0b6362acc05aea4fde2c815d3c4`,
and `8540a680ad35a30083076031a47bb08d830bff97511bba5df1853798689456c2`.

The hull-only direct path costs 0.301 microseconds per BREP shot in the
production profile, down from 0.559 microseconds for the initial general
coefficient construction.  Seven alternating cache-disabled 1024-square
trials give median throughput of 475,949.39 rays/CPU-second with the probe off
and 462,819.75 with it on.  Probe-on is 97.24 percent of probe-off, a
2.84-percent CPU-time increment, and preserves the exact branch-legacy image.
The root-independent first stage therefore passes the preferred five-percent
incremental budget even though it must remain evidence-only.

The viable extension is a two-stage missing-root route, not direct dispatch
from the broad hull.  First traverse the prepared candidate hierarchy
independently of legacy leaves and bind each feature cell to exact prepared
span indices and outward normalized restrictions.  Status-2 mappings must be
proved or remain unsupported.  Then apply a bounded trim-aware root-count and
classification certificate to hull-positive spans, reusing immutable
boundary/derivative packs from a depth/configuration-specific cache variant.
Only a complete witness of a material root absent from the legacy ledger may
request the atomic prepared transaction; every failure remains whole-ray
legacy.  This design has a measured affordable first stage and an already
qualified atomic publication mechanism.  Its open risks are positive-corpus
coverage and second-stage selectivity, not a fundamental routing or 63-face
speed barrier.

The complete optimized Ninja build succeeds at this checkpoint.  The expanded
focused BREP/audit/cache matrix passes 85/85 in 37.86 seconds at four-way
CTest concurrency, including the permanent missing-root probe and the direct
probe-on byte-identity regression.


2026-09-30 IMPORTED ROUTING CAPACITY DIAGNOSTIC AND SUCCESS DECISION

Verdict: the routing redesign is successful for its current certified
boundary/contact class, and no fundamental 63-face performance, feature-cell,
depth-cache, NURBS, or topology obstacle has been found.  The remaining blocks
to default authority are correction-class coverage, an independent
missing-root signal with qualified selectivity, representative imported render
timing, and sanitizers;
they are qualification and scope limits rather than evidence that the routing
architecture must be abandoned.

The installed NIST 1--6 and 11 sweeps each complete 118 rays with zero depth
differences, corrections, missed corrections, reversals, or feature-cell
lookup failures.  NIST 7--10 adds four much larger objects and 472 rays.  Its
first run reported 236 apparent lookup failures, all on `Solid1.s` and
`Solid1_3.s`.  Reason-specific diagnostics prove that all 236 were
`unmatched_root` observations after the fixed legacy leaf workspace had
overflowed.  Metadata availability, null/nonleaf pointers, face indices, leaf
ordinals, record ranges, and stored cell identities each had exactly zero
failures.  The fixed list deliberately retains only a bounded prefix, while
the allocating legacy fallback observes roots from the complete leaf list;
those later roots cannot be mapped by scanning the prefix.  `leaf_overflow`
already makes the whole route unsupported, launches no optional work, and
retains legacy authority, so treating these expected omissions as metadata
lookup failures was a telemetry-category defect rather than an unsafe routing
decision.

The route decision now records eight genuine lookup reasons separately and
reports overflow-only omissions as
`roots_unmapped_after_leaf_overflow`, outside the lookup-failure total.  The
corpus driver carries that distinction into its CSV and manifest.  The fresh
archive
`brlcad_build/brep-routing-corpus-nist7-10-redesign`
passes all four objects and 472 rays with zero cell lookup failures, zero
prepared/legacy or depth differences, zero reversal mismatches, and zero
missed corrections.  It records the same 236 expected overflow omissions,
14 leaf-workspace fallback rays, and 12 hit-workspace fallback rays.  Per
object, `Solid1.s` records 116 omissions on four leaf-fallback rays and
`Solid1_3.s` records 120 on six; `Solid1_1.s` and `Solid1_2.s` record none.
Manifest, summary, and JSONL SHA-256 values are respectively

2026-09-30 EVALUATE-ONCE CERTIFIED-ROOT LEDGER

Milestone 1(h) and cost-reduction checkpoint 7 now retain each accepted local
root's outward source-box t interval, NURBS and original-surface UVs, evaluated
surface point, first derivatives, oriented unit normal, trim classification,
and topology indices in the fixed per-ray ledger.  Duplicate corrector seeds
are recognized before original-surface evaluation and union their outward t
intervals instead of evaluating the same root again.  A singular derivative
cross product retains the established normal fallback without treating the
derivative-validity and normal-validity claims as the same fact.

Candidate cleanup, grouped-event cleanup, and seam-existing-root construction
reuse the retained mapped UV and normal.  Final physical-event publication
uses them only when the event still names the exact local source root and its
face, span, distance, UV, trim/classification, adjacency, and direction all
match.  Any mismatch takes the established surface-evaluation path; singular-
pole overrides remain unchanged.  The trace records cached and fallback final-
hit evaluations separately.  This is per-ray state and does not alter the
serialized BRP6 prep-cache payload or variant.

The permanent `rt_brep_ray_evaluation_cache` test shoots an oblique converted
sphere and requires two complete regular events.  Both retained roots have
finite mapped UV, point, derivatives, normal, and enclosing t interval; their
points lie on the ray, and cached normal dots reproduce the stored directions.
The final result reports two cached hit evaluations and zero fallback surface
evaluations.  The complete optimized Ninja build succeeds, and the expanded
focused BREP/audit/cache matrix passes 84/84 in 38.10 seconds.  Immutable
derivative/boundary/error prep packs and representative STEP/FRAME and
sanitizer qualification remain open.

2026-09-30 PLATE-MODE CONTRACT COMPLETION

Milestone 12(b) is complete.  Plate thickness parsing now rejects empty,
malformed, overflow, NaN, and infinite attributes and maps valid signed input
to a finite nonnegative thickness.  Line-of-sight construction validates the
hit, ray, normal, and thickness; accounts for non-unit normals and ray
directions; preserves `nocos`; and rejects zero-length or grazing input rather
than emitting nonfinite segments.  Entry normals are normalized and oriented
against the ray in both ray directions, with the exit normal exactly opposite.
The fixed and allocating paths return the number of segments actually emitted,
and plate mode remains excluded from prepared solid authority with the explicit
`RT_BREP_PREPARED_FALLBACK_PLATE` reason.

The permanent `rt_brep_ray_plate` analytic test uses an open planar disk and
covers cosine and `nocos` thickness, perpendicular/oblique/reversed rays,
identity/rigid/uniform-scale/reflection/nonuniform transforms, exact and
near-grazing behavior, normal orientation, fixed-versus-trace equivalence,
prepared fallback, and invalid attribute parsing.  The optimized Ninja build
succeeds and the expanded focused BREP/cache matrix passes 81/81.  The
telemetry checkpoint above advances 12(c); reachability proof and
evidence-gated legacy cleanup retirement remain open.


2026-09-30 PRINCIPAL CURVATURE CONTRACT COMPLETION

Milestone 12(a) is complete.  `rt_brep_curve` now evaluates the hit face at the
certified hit UV through `ON_Surface::Ev2Der`, supplies the normalized
face-oriented hit normal to `ON_EvPrincipalCurvatures`, and publishes the
smaller-absolute principal curvature and its tangent direction as `crv_c1` and
`crv_pdir`, preserving librt's `|crv_c1| <= |crv_c2|` contract.  Using the hit
normal rather than the surface's parameter normal gives the documented outward-
normal curvature sign for reversed BREP faces.  Invalid face indices, UVs,
normals, derivative evaluations, or principal frames return zero curvatures and
a deterministic unit tangent rather than partially initialized output.

The permanent `rt_brep_ray_curve` analytic test covers converted sphere,
cylinder, planar ARB8 faces, and a rotated nonuniform ellipsoid in both ray
directions.  It also verifies reversed oriented-normal signs and invalid face,
UV, and singular-normal fallbacks.  At that checkpoint the optimized Ninja
build succeeded and the focused BREP/cache matrix passed 80/80; the plate-mode
checkpoint above raises the active matrix to 81 tests.


2026-09-30 ATOMIC CONTACT-GATED SELECTIVE ROUTING DETERMINATION

Verdict: yes, routing can be redesigned successfully for the correction class
that is presently observable and independently certified.  The current
implementation removes the 63-face performance failure without weakening the
fail-closed transaction: legacy runs once and is retained untouched, while
optional verification/prepared work is selected independently.  Direct mode
retains legacy authority.  Adding the separate
`LIBRT_BREP_ROUTE_PUBLISH=1` opt-in allows only a complete, supported,
correction-risk prepared transaction to replace it before segment emission.
Default behavior is unchanged.  This is not yet a default-production-authority
decision; missing-root and other unobserved correction classes, representative
STEP/FRAME qualification, and the sanitizer gate remain open.  The shared
reversal defects found by the routing sweeps are now closed.

The final corpus-derived selector requests `complex_prepared` only for a
structurally supported, within-capacity, nonempty legacy stream where every
stored root is hazardous, at least one root is a numerical trim contact, and
an intersected prep cell carries the boundary/contact-correction bit.  A leaf,
root, metadata, or lookup-capacity failure now retains legacy immediately and
requests no optional work, because the prepared publication gate could not
authorize that result.  All other observations also request `none`;
`seeded_ordinary` remains implemented but is not selected.  Empty and mixed
clean/hazard streams deliberately retain legacy with no optional work.  This
conjunction is important: broad topology, generic `UNQUALIFIED`, an all-hazard
classification alone, a contact alone, or an unsupported observation no longer
charges the expensive solver.  Audit strata are classified independently of
the selected lane, so route recall cannot be made tautological by the policy
itself.

Atomic publication is now implemented for this certified boundary/contact
class.  All ten permanent correction-pair replays publish the prepared oracle
result.  Selective publication deliberately accepts only a complete empty
partition whose proof retained no surface root box.  The first retained box,
or any nonempty completed partition, rejects the semantic event class and
falls through to the untouched legacy transaction.  This is not a timing or
sampled-work cutoff, and the unrestricted prepared solver is unchanged.

On the complete 708-ray depth-eight 63-face audit, 38 rays request complex

2026-09-29 ORTHOGONAL SELECTIVE ROUTING FEASIBILITY CHECKPOINT

Verdict: yes, the routing transaction can be redesigned successfully.  The
current shadow classifier and opt-in direct implementation remove the
structural performance failure of the earlier broad three-lane classifier,
retain the complete legacy
transaction, and finds every independently certified correction in the

The policy is now represented by three orthogonal fields rather than by a lane
that also implies authority:

  * base transaction: legacy;
  * optional work: `none`, `seeded_ordinary`, or `complex_prepared`; and
  * publication authority: legacy.

The decision reuses the fixed SurfaceTree leaves already visited by the
legacy solve, depth-specific prep-time feature cells, and a fixed-capacity
ray-local root-hazard ledger.  It needs no second face traversal, allocation,
statistical work cap, or timing decision.  Generic `UNQUALIFIED` geometry no
longer implies expensive work.  With complete metadata, complex work is chosen
for the observed correction class only when every stored legacy root is a
boundary/contact hazard.  Structural lookup/capacity failures also choose the
conservative complex path.  Empty root streams and mixed clean/hazard streams
remain unqualified, retain legacy authority, and do no optional work.  The
seeded ordinary verifier remains implemented but is not selected by the
current corpus-derived policy.

not routing mechanics.  A router based on observed legacy roots cannot infer a
