  failed ownership attempts are accounted box failures and publish nothing.
  Eight contact pairs have two source boxes and roots each and one allowable
  raw half-open miss-side root each.  The experimental local cleanup had two
  ambiguous comparisons only when both gap and clearance exceeded the model
  tolerance by 10%; no ambiguity occurred inside tolerance and no comparison
  was an oracle regression.  Finding 84's event-local near-miss cleanup
  removes both remaining ambiguities without changing the selection,
  fallback, or seam-pair populations.  A 280-selection floor preserves the
  conservative fallback increase as an explicit performance/certification
  ratchet.

    ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_brep_ray($|_)' \
      -E '^rt_brep_ray_seam$' -j14 --output-on-failure

  PASS: 18/18 in 139.15 seconds.  The exact NIST pair 1 and UCP pairs 0, 1, 3,
  and 43 pass depth-three/eight partition and reversal comparison.  All seven
  optimized serial/parallel cache tests pass after removal of per-BBNode trim
  pointer lists and legacy CurveTree leaf-key serialization.

  Final ASAN+UBSAN validation on rebuilt binaries, with leak scanning disabled
  for the established host ptrace limitation:

    env ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0:halt_on_error=1 \
      UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1:print_stacktrace=1 \
      ctest --test-dir .build-asan \
        -R '^(rt_brep_ray_(trim_classifier|defect|nonisoparametric|contact_trim|cleanup)|rt_cache_.*)$' \
        -j4 --output-on-failure

  PASS: 12/12 in 202.82 seconds with no sanitizer diagnostic.

  Correctness remains ahead of optimization, but the next performance work is
  now concrete.  Recover the 42 selections lost to conservative certification
  only with general proofs; profile normal production separately from trace;
  then pursue the Abert-informed object/face/span BVH, compact immutable data,
  conservative SIMD rejection, batched vshot, and corrector/evaluation work if
  measured hot.  Preserve directed implicit-to-BREP partitions, paired
  Cauchy-Crofton area/volume convergence, grazing ladders, crack perturbations,
  similarity/reversal tests, and the difficult-root C/C++ corpus as hard gates.

  Imported agreement remains evidence of invariant behavior, not a geometric
  oracle.  The stronger truth program is unchanged: analytic implicit-to-BREP
  directed partitions, paired implicit/BREP Cauchy-Crofton area and volume,
  signed grazing ladders converging toward point tangency, Cobb seam/crack
  perturbations, scale/translation/rotation invariance, and later CSG-tree
  substitution in both directions.  Keep the production depth at eight while
  completing those gates.  The topology-aware cell theorem can be optimized;
  it must not be replaced by unrestricted endpoint fuzz or closest-trim
  orientation.


Local near-miss resolution and reversal-invariant balanced runs (Finding 84):

  The 848-face `IBEX_SOLID_item.s` in
  `original/SC_solid_stp_20260429.g` completed the sampled ray comparison for
  the largest model in the initial 14-database preparation stratum.  Its
  deterministic pair 22 has origin

    (95.848591855513888, -55.740815264982629,
     -11.3876265194396)

  direction

    (-0.58941604087522914, 0.74193704918254422,
     0.31955929936283961)

  and chord `255.39858798473995`.  Fresh adaptive-depth-three and depth-eight
  trees found the same nine roots.  Before repair the forward legacy path
  returned no intervals and the reversed path returned two.  Prepared tracing
  also failed closed with six unresolved events, so it did not conceal or
  supply the fallback answer.

  Raw cleanup telemetry isolated two direction-sensitive policies.  First,
  NEAR_MISS cleanup used sorted adjacency and matching normal direction as if
  they proved one physical event.  Forward cleanup let a leaving near miss at
  about 71.715 interact with a clean entry at about 75.263 despite their
  several-unit separation.  Reversal changed ordering and retained a
  different stream.  Near-miss versus non-miss removal, consecutive near-miss
  merging, and near-miss versus clean/near-hit relabeling now all require
  `brep_solid_events_coincident` using the established model-derived parameter
  envelope.  The fixed and allocating paths implement the same locality rule.

  Second, the old solid winding code ignored leading unmatched exits but
  deleted the whole suffix after an unmatched trailing entry.  A stream
  equivalent to ENTER, ENTER, ENTER, LEAVE, LEAVE consequently lost its
  balanced interior in one direction while the reversed form retained it.
  Cleanup now reverse-scans to remove only entries with no later exit, then
  forward-scans to remove only exits with no earlier retained entry.  This is
  allocation-free parenthesis matching.  The surviving stream is compacted
  to the same nonzero-winding union intervals as before.  A lone original
  observation remains available to the certified crack/closure repair.

  A new source test helper accepts complete synthetic hit-class streams.
  `--cleanup-only` ratchets both ENTER/ENTER/ENTER/LEAVE/LEAVE and the reduced
  SC stream, including a locally coincident near-hit/near-miss pair and a
  distant adjacent near-miss/clean pair.  Each test supplies the exact
  reversed stream and compares the full retained partition under
  `t_reverse = chord - t_forward`.  It is deliberately C++ data rather than a
  model-specific `.g` fixture and joins the difficult-case corpus required to
  prevent fixed-this/broke-that solver work.

  Exact pair 22 now returns two intervals in both directions and at both
  depths:

    [58.107629653..., 71.715107548...]
    [93.746252051..., 184.158146005...]

  The complete 70-ray legacy-only SC comparison passes with 16 hit rays, 54
  misses, 26 segments, zero invalid/orientation/depth/reversal differences,
  and maximum depth delta `3.3064736726373667e-8`.  The final depth-three tree
  has 270,839 nodes and 172,669 leaves; depth eight has 3,494,794 nodes and
  1,787,007 leaves; both use 60,527 CurveTree leaves.  Peak resident memory was
  about 973 MB.

  The full prepared-plus-legacy 70-ray trace also passes.  Both requested
  depths report 35 eligible/attempted rays, zero selections, 31 uncertified
  fallbacks, two surface-box overflows, and two event-class fallbacks.  Each
  reports 2,498 candidate spans, 18,336 subdivision boxes, 1,235 isolated
  boxes, 213 fold attempts, eight fold certificates, 18 surface-box
  overflows, 35 physically complete ledgers, and 225 unresolved physical
  events.  Wall time was 7m43.79s, CPU time 559.33s, and peak RSS 950,184 KB
  on this host.  This is trace instrumentation, not a controlled normal-shot
  benchmark, but it makes the general optimization target clear: reduce
  object/face/span candidate traversal and proof work before assuming Newton
  iteration is the primary cost.

  Optimized validation after the repair is 18/18 non-seam ray shards in
  150.68 seconds plus a complete passing seam shard.  The seam matrix retains
  280 selections, 136 uncertified fallbacks, 24 seam pairs, zero leaks, and
  14/14 known crack repairs.  Its formerly two out-of-tolerance experimental
  local ambiguities are now zero because distant sorted events cannot modify
  one another.  Rebuilt ASAN/UBSAN `--cleanup-only` passes with no diagnostic;
  the exact high-memory SC replay was qualified in the optimized build rather
  than multiplied under ASAN.

  This repair improves the tolerant legacy fallback; it is not prepared
  authority.  The prepared ledger still fails the whole ray on incomplete
  ownership, unresolved event identity, unsupported trim/seam topology, or
  nonzero final winding.  Parameter coincidence prevents a known local crack
  observation from corrupting an otherwise usable fallback but does not prove
  arbitrary roots equivalent.

  Correctness remains the immediate priority.  Production adaptive depth
  stays eight while analytic implicit-to-BREP partitions, paired
  Cauchy-Crofton area/volume convergence, signed grazing ladders, Cobb crack
  perturbations, similarity/reversal invariance, feature-targeted imported
  rays, and later CSG-tree substitutions expand.  Once these answers and
  fallback boundaries are stable, the performance phase should profile normal
  production, prioritize a compact object/face/span BVH and conservative
  batched rejection, implement a real `vshot`, and optimize OpenNURBS
  evaluation/Newton correction only when measured hot.  The goal is a broadly
  applicable certified pipeline, not exact reproduction of Abert et al.


Feature-targeted chords and coplanar grazing cleanup (Finding 85):

  `brep-audit --ray-depth-compare` now accepts
  `--feature-ray-count=N`.  After the stable three axis and requested random
  pairs it appends a stratified mix of face-domain-midpoint normal chords,
  edge-midpoint face-normal chords, and edge-midpoint face-conormal chords.
  The last category is locally tangent to the incident face and transverse to
  the edge.  Points, tangents, normals, and conormals come directly from BREP
  surfaces and trims, not a tessellation.  Bounding-sphere endpoints keep the
  finite chord outside the object envelope.

  Output separately records requested and actual random/feature populations,
  every feature category, target face and edge, sample kind, and the exact
  global pair line.  Existing `--ray-pair` replay remains stable for axis and
  random pairs when feature count is zero; with feature sampling enabled it
  selects the recorded global index.  Invalid domains, singular evaluations,
  and targets outside the envelope are skipped.  Available feature counts can
  therefore be below the request and are never padded with invented geometry.

  These are coverage and invariance probes, not imported geometric truth.
  The underlying surface midpoint may be trimmed away, and a local edge graze
  may correctly be a contact-only miss.  The applicable assertions remain
  complete finite oriented partitions, fresh depth-three/depth-eight equality,
  and reversal invariance.  Analytic implicit-to-BREP partitions and paired
  Cauchy-Crofton convergence retain oracle authority.

  The first feature run targeted `Product_step18_item.s` in
  `non_grabcad/0353/0353-02-57.g`: eight face-normal, eight edge-normal, and
  eight edge-graze pairs plus three axis controls, for 54 directed rays.  Its
  earlier 64-random-pair run passed, but the targeted run initially found five
  differing rays, five candidate-depth reversal failures, and seven reference-
  depth reversal failures.  Maximum partition disagreement was 2.5 model
  units.

  One class was the odd NEAR_MISS endpoint heuristic left ahead of Finding
  84's symmetric matcher.  Pair 10 placed two coincident entry observations on
  a seam.  One depth classified them NEAR_HIT/NEAR_HIT and the other
  NEAR_HIT/NEAR_MISS.  The old odd-count stage discarded a distant outer
  boundary before the first representation could coalesce.  Removing raw
  front/back deletion from fixed and allocating cleanup leaves local event
  identity and balanced matching to make the decision.  A reduced C++ stream
  now changes only the coincident near-side representative and requires the
  same outer partition under reversal.

  Pair 11 exposed a distinct dense-tree continuum.  Its edge-normal line is
  coplanar with face 11.  Depth three reported six raw observations and the
  correct 0.8-unit interval.  Depth eight reported 130 raw observations, 126
  after near cleanup, including many CLEAN_HIT samples with zero normal dot
  along the face.  Cleanup retained one arbitrary sample next to a transverse
  entry and published a 0.025-unit interval; reversal selected the opposite
  end of the continuum.

  The fixed grazing loop intentionally copied a historical allocating-list
  iterator bug: erasing the first item advanced past the new first item.  Both
  containers now test the returned item immediately.  More importantly,
  `brep_resolved_grazing_pair` now enforces the premise recorded in Finding 48:
  both endpoints, not only the currently inspected one, must be grazing before
  ordered separation can preserve an affine-grazing material interval.  A
  zero-normal contact cannot borrow authority from a transverse entry.  The
  existing trim, direction, finite-distance, and minimum-size conditions are
  unchanged.  A pure C++ stream with three zero-normal coplanar samples between
  ordinary entry and exit requires all three to disappear.

  Final feature replay passes every pair with zero depth-partition and reversal
  differences and maximum endpoint delta
  `5.3813607792108087e-8`.  Pair 11 returns
  `[19.511658187986882,20.31165818798695]` in both depths and its exact reversed
  interval.  The depth-eight allocating trace still records 130 raw roots and
  a 64-root trace capacity overflow, but cleanup reduces the full list to the
  same two physical endpoints; production's fixed hit workspace falls back to
  that allocating path on capacity overflow.

  Pair 9 has one explicitly non-semantic face-identity difference.  Coincident
  faces 151--154 contribute the same two-unit interval.  Depth-three reverse
  cleanup retains face 151 at entry; depth eight retains a face-152 root about
  5.7e-14 earlier.  Both normals and endpoints agree and both map to the
  face-151 forward exit.  `face_difference_ray_indices` now records exact
  examples and includes them in diagnostic records.  This is a prepared
  topological-ownership target, not permission to call different material
  partitions equivalent.

  The registered converted-sphere audit now requests all three feature types.
  Exact feature pair 5 replay records an edge graze with four raw seam roots
  and no material interval in either direction.  The focused affine gate
  passes all 78 directed trend rays after requiring two grazing endpoints.
  The complete non-seam optimized gate passes 18/18 in 176.33 seconds.  The
  Cobb local comparison is tightened from its obsolete two-promotion floor to
  zero regressions and zero ambiguous partition changes: the general legacy
  cleanup now supplies the two formerly promoted correct answers.  The final
  seam run passes all 416 matrix rays with 16/16 crack repairs, zero leaks,
  zero reversal inconsistencies, 280 prepared selections, 136 conservative
  fallbacks, and 24 certified seam pairs.  Rebuilt ASAN/UBSAN `--cleanup-only`
  and feature-enabled `brep_audit_ray_depth_compare` pass with no diagnostic.

  Add feature populations to every subsequent imported manifest result.  Use
  exact selected-pair replay and reduce every failure to a source stream or
  small analytic BREP before changing production behavior.  Continue random
  chords for broad occupancy, signed grazing ladders for tangent-limit truth,
  Cauchy-Crofton sampling for integral convergence, and Cobb perturbations for
  crack policy; no one sampling mode substitutes for the others.


Transverse ownership of coincident coplanar roots (Finding 86):

  The next independent feature-ray slice used four Recurta AP203 solids that
  were previously measured only by the prep census.  Each run used fresh
  adaptive-depth-three and depth-eight trees, the legacy solver, three axis
  pairs, and a stratified total of 12 feature pairs: four face-normal, four
  edge-normal, and four edge-grazing pairs.  Every chord was shot in both
  directions.  Three objects passed immediately:

    * `10007.g/id2_item.s`, 15 faces, passes all 30 rays with maximum endpoint
      delta `3.1835818425918205e-9`.  One reverse edge-normal endpoint changes
      owning seam face without changing the material partition.
    * `10062.g/id2_item.s`, 3,623 faces, passes all 30 rays with maximum
      endpoint delta `5.504829658775634e-8` and no face-owner difference.
    * `10088.g/id2_item.s`, 5,771 faces, passes all 30 rays with maximum
      endpoint delta `6.6577683810464805e-8` and no face-owner difference.

  `10057.g/id2_item.s`, a 301-face solid, initially had no depth-dependent
  partition difference but failed reversal at both depths on global pair 3.
  This face-normal chord targets face 37 and has origin

    (-4.25, -42.493022533117163, -96)

  direction `(0,1,0)`, and chord length `84.986045066234325`.  The forward
  solve returned no material while reversal returned a 9.7-unit interval.
  Both depths produced the same underlying roots, so this was neither a tree
  density problem nor a nonlinear-solve discrepancy.

  At each physical endpoint, one near-trim root was transverse to the ray and
  one or more roots came from planar faces containing the ray.  A coplanar
  root has zero normal dot, but `brep_hit::direction` must still contain one of
  ENTERING or LEAVING and historically obtains the LEAVING value from the
  nonnegative sign of zero.  Early NEAR_MISS cleanup compared that arbitrary
  bit before the grazing stage.  In one ray order it paired a transverse
  NEAR_MISS with a coincident zero-normal root and erased the real boundary;
  reversal changed the arbitrary comparison and retained it.

  Solid cleanup now establishes local event authority before any near-trim
  pairing.  Within one model-distance event cluster, if at least one
  observation is transverse, the fixed workspace and allocating list both
  remove coincident observations whose normal dot is within
  `BREP_GRAZING_DOT_TOL`.  The transverse witness owns the material state
  transition.  Entirely grazing clusters are unchanged, as are separated
  entering/leaving grazing pairs and all plate/open-surface cleanup.  The
  cluster envelope is the existing model-space parameter tolerance, including
  ray-direction scaling and floating-point slack; sorted adjacency alone does
  not establish identity.  Production remains allocation-free on the fixed
  path.

  A pure C++ stream at `src/librt/tests/brep_ray.cpp:1079` reduces the imported
  arrangement to a transverse NEAR_MISS plus coincident zero-normal NEAR_MISS
  and NEAR_HIT shadows at each endpoint.  It requires the same interval after
  reversal.  The production helpers are at
  `src/librt/primitives/brep/brep.cpp:13918` and `:13957`; both are called before
  near-miss cleanup.  Exact imported pair 3 now returns
  `[35.643022533117161,45.343022533117164]` forward and
  `[39.643022533117161,49.343022533117164]` in reverse at both depths, with
  zero fixed/list discrepancy on those rays.  The complete 15-pair object run
  passes all 30 rays, has no face-owner difference, and has maximum depth delta
  `2.9734749773524527e-8`.

  This slice also sharpens the prep scaling problem.  The depth-three and
  depth-eight results respectively retained:

    * `10007`: 1,484/61,268 surface leaves and 37,016 curve leaves, 75.2 MiB
      process peak;
    * `10062`: 8,916/48,170 surface leaves and 118,694 curve leaves, 348.1 MiB
      process peak;
    * `10088`: 9,373/52,213 surface leaves and 108,433 curve leaves, 310.0 MiB
      process peak; and
    * `10057`: 6,603/315,415 surface leaves and 482,684 curve leaves at either
      surface depth, 585.0 MiB process peak.

  Curve-tree population does not respond to the adaptive surface-depth knob.
  On `10057`, curve leaves outnumber shallow surface leaves by 73 to 1 and even
  outnumber dense surface leaves.  In the first concurrent high-face-count
  pass, end-to-end prep wall time was about 44--48 seconds at depth three and
  71 seconds at depth eight, while reported surface-tree build time was only
  0.88 and 15--17 seconds.  A repeat under different concurrent scheduling
  reported about 21--30 seconds of wall time and 0.31--5.95 seconds of surface
  build time.  Do not treat either concurrent pass as a serial benchmark; the
  variability itself requires isolated phase and allocation counters.  In
  every run, substantial wall work is outside the reported surface build and
  the curve population is unchanged.  These measurements make CurveTree
  design, per-face setup, and retained/temporary ownership part of the
  required prep-memory audit; surface-tree compaction alone cannot solve
  scaling.

  Qualification is green.  `rt_brep_ray --cleanup-only` passes.  The optimized
  non-seam gate passes 18/18 in 168.01 seconds.  The 416-ray seam gate passes in
  251.46 seconds.  Rebuilt ASAN/UBSAN cleanup-only and feature-enabled
  `brep_audit_ray_depth_compare` pass with leak scanning disabled for the
  established host-runtime limitation and no sanitizer diagnostic.


Compact CurveTree ownership and cache layout (Finding 87):

  The memory audit found that `bu_heap` is a size-classed, per-
  `bu_parallel_id()` bump allocator whose small-object `bu_heap_put` path is a
  no-op.  `PooledObject<T>` routed CurveTree, BRNode, BBNode, and their
  separately allocated STL holders through it.  Deleted per-face trees
  therefore left all small-object pages resident.  Adding an ordinary free
  list is not a safe local fix: returned objects do not carry allocating-
  thread/page ownership, destruction may occur on another thread, and page
  liveness/reclamation is undefined.  `src/libbu/heap.c` now has a detailed
  TODO for those generic requirements.  BREP bulk-lifetime storage must not
  depend on that future design.

  Production CurveTree trim classification never traversed its recursive
  binary BRNode construction hierarchy.  It used only the prepared per-loop
  leaf indices.  Curve subdivision now preserves the same knot, horizontal/
  vertical-tangent, `isLinear`, and maximum-depth decisions but appends only
  terminal intervals to a CurveTree-owned vector.  Internal BRNodes and their
  child vectors are not retained.  The old global sorted pointer vector is
  gone, and each loop stores only the fixed-coordinate ordering selected by
  its surface's one parity-ray orientation rather than duplicate X and Y
  indices.

  The compact BRNode keeps its source `ON_Curve`, parameter interval, UV
  sampled bounding rectangle, UV endpoints, trim/adjacent-face indices, and
  four classification flags.  Exact crossing and proximity methods still
  evaluate the native curve.  It is 104 bytes rather than the prior 232-byte
  object plus a separately allocated 24-byte STL holder.  A periodic leaf
  image is 16 bytes and stores a checked 32-bit leaf index, two integer image
  numbers, and endpoint-extension bits; offsets are reconstructed from the
  immutable surface periods.  `CurveTree`, its holder, and its leaf vectors
  now have ordinary owning lifetimes and release storage normally.

  Prep-cache version 2 serializes the flat leaf population and recorded
  construction depth.  Version-1 recursive records are rejected.  A compact
  leaf needs 89 serialized bytes and no internal-node record.  Both serial and
  parallel BREP sphere cache round-trip tests pass.

  Recurta `10057.g/id2_item.s` retains 482,684 curve leaves, 6,603 shallow
  surface leaves, and 315,415 depth-eight surface leaves.  Its exact 15-pair,
  two-depth feature audit now peaks at 372,117,504 bytes versus the preceding
  approximately 585.0-MiB checkpoint, about a 230-MiB/39-percent reduction.  All
  30 rays pass with no reversal, disposition, partition, or face-owner
  difference; maximum depth delta remains `2.9734749773524527e-8`.  The serial
  depth-three per-face profile falls from 205,426,688 to 164,495,360 bytes and
  completes in 1.56 seconds on the final warm repetition.  Timing varied
  substantially between repetitions and is not yet a formal benchmark.

  The high-topology `10088.g/id2_item.s` check covers 5,771 faces, 108,433
  curve leaves, and 52,213 deep surface leaves.  All 30 feature rays pass with
  maximum delta `6.6577683810464805e-8`; peak is 283,979,776 bytes versus the
  prior approximate 310.0-MiB checkpoint.  Its smaller percentage reduction
  is consistent with OpenNURBS topology and imported-model storage dominating
  more strongly than CurveTree leaves.

  Qualification also passes the trim-classifier, cleanup, endpoint, contact-
  trim, distorted-defect, cache, and 416-ray seam suites.  The representation
  change does not alter leaf counts, curve intervals, trim tolerance, or exact
  native-curve solving.

  SurfaceTree storage is now the next major prep-memory target.  The deep
  10057 run retains 618,445 BBNodes.  At this checkpoint every BBNode still
  uses process-lifetime pooled allocation and a separate holder; Finding 88
  removes the holder while preserving the tree.  The subsequent design must
  pack child relationships into compact immutable/indexed nodes or transfer a
  per-prep arena into `brep_specific`.  Structural seam/knot splits, parallel
  ownership, fresh/cache identity, and allocation-free shooting remain
  mandatory.  Whole-model OpenNURBS topology, immutable span sharing, vector
  capacity, cache streaming, and smaller per-object savings must also be
  attributed because large top-level STEP assemblies will combine all costs.


Compact BBNode child ownership and all-span scaling (Finding 88):

  The first SurfaceTree memory step replaces every separately allocated,
  pooled 24-byte `BBNode::Stl` vector holder with a directly owned child-buffer
  pointer and 32-bit count/capacity.  The buffer supports two/four-way surface
  nodes and the many-face object root, preserves insertion and traversal order,
  and is normally freed.  The cache's recursive child-count/preorder format is
  unchanged.  `sizeof(BBNode)` rises from 152 to 160 bytes, but eliminating the
  holder reduces every childless leaf from 176 to 160 retained bytes before
  allocator metadata and removes one allocation from every internal node.
  `BBNode` itself remains on the unreclaimed generic pool pending a per-prep
  arena or immutable indexed/build-then-pack representation.

  Recurta `10057.g/id2_item.s` retains identical populations at both depths:
  618,445 nodes/315,415 leaves at depth eight, 12,107 nodes/6,603 leaves at
  depth three, and 482,684 curve leaves.  Its exact 30-ray prepared-and-legacy
  feature comparison passes with no depth, reversal, disposition, partition,
  or face-owner difference and the unchanged
  `2.9734749773524527e-8` maximum delta.  Peak falls from 372,117,504 to
  363,761,664 bytes, an 8,355,840-byte (7.97-MiB, 2.25-percent) incremental
  reduction.

  The 5,771-face `10088.g/id2_item.s` depth-eight prep succeeds in 17.24
  seconds at 236,576,768 bytes.  Its complete 30-ray legacy-only comparison
  passes with 96,787/12,387 deep/shallow nodes, 52,213/9,373 leaves, zero
  invalid rays, zero partition or reversal differences, and the unchanged
  `6.6577683810464805e-8` maximum delta; peak is 281,817,088 bytes.  Cache,
  trim-classifier, cleanup, endpoint, and the complete 416-ray seam gate also
  pass.  The seam gate takes 229.89 seconds.  Rebuilt ASan/UBSan serial and
  parallel cache destruction plus cleanup pass 3/3 with leak scanning disabled
  for the established host-runtime limitation and no sanitizer diagnostic.

  The seam time is not 416 ordinary rays through one prepared solid.  The
  bowed-Cobb portion builds 16 distorted variants and performs two BREP preps
  per variant.  Each of its 416 matrix positions runs one implicit shot, two
  production BREP shots for determinism, and one instrumented BREP trace: 1,664
  calls, 1,248 of them BREP.  The trace itself runs prepared certification and
  the legacy path, observes all 12 manifold edges, and records adaptive gap,
  correspondence, trim, local-root, closure, and continuation evidence.  The
  shard also runs thirteen other seam/vertex/contact/defect corpora.  Its Cobb
  geometry has only six faces/spans, so this is not 10088's missing span BVH.
  Add an already-prepared non-trace throughput fixture; do not quote the proof
  matrix wall time as production ray latency.

  A full prepared-and-legacy 10088 audit was stopped after more than ten
  minutes.  Selected axis pair zero alone exceeded a two-minute bound, while
  its legacy-only form completed correctly and reported only 13/19 intersected
  leaves and 26/38 solver calls at shallow/deep depth.  The cost is the
  independent prepared path: `brep_trace_surface_spans` scans every algebraic
  span in all 5,771 faces and interval-isolates every coefficient-hull
  candidate without using the BBNode hierarchy.  This confirms the planned
  conservative object/face/span BVH as a high-value performance requirement.
  Keep prep-memory benchmarks and hierarchy qualification separate from that
  diagnostic all-span cost; use bounded selected prepared traces until an
  object-level span index exists.


Reclaimable per-face SurfaceTree arenas and seam profiling (Finding 89):

  `BBNode` no longer inherits the process-lifetime `PooledObject` allocator.
  Each `SurfaceTree` now owns a `BBNodeArena` during construction.  The first
  face node is an ordinary anchor suitable for the existing raw-root transfer
  API; later nodes use placement construction in blocks of 4, 16, 64, and at
  most 256 nodes.  If knot partitioning makes the final logical root differ
  from the first node, its fields and child storage are adopted into the
  anchor.  Finishing attaches the arena to that one transferable root.  The
  ordinary object-level BVH root recursively owns all face roots, so deleting
  `brep_specific` now destroys and frees every retained surface-node block.

  Bounds, UV intervals, estimates, normals, trim flags, split topology, child
  order, closed-seam and internal-knot splits, and traversal are unchanged.
  Cache serialization is byte-compatible; cache loading now uses the same
  per-face arena representation.  The ordinary recursive deserializer remains
  available for API compatibility.  Partial tree failures are reclaimed in
  bulk rather than relying on error-site deletion of selected parents and
  children.

  ASan/UBSan found a use-after-free in the first arena teardown: the newest
  block was freed first even though parents are allocated before children and
  a parent destructor still inspects child ownership.  Teardown now restores
  block allocation order and destroys parents before children while all child
  storage is live.  Cache serial/parallel destruction, cleanup, endpoint, and
  `--local-root-only` all pass under ASan/UBSan.  The latter includes the
  deliberate `FAILURE_NORMAL_EVALUATION` construction failure, directly
  exercising partial-arena cleanup.  Focused release tests pass 12/12;
  `brep_audit_prep_repeat` and the all-face SurfaceTree audit pass 2/2.

  `sizeof(BBNode)` remains 160 bytes at alignment eight.  The full 10057
  depth-three/eight comparison preserves 618,445/12,107 nodes,
  315,415/6,603 leaves, 482,684 curve leaves, all 30 ray answers, and the
  `2.9734749773524527e-8` maximum endpoint delta.  Its observed process peak
  is 359,940,096 bytes versus 363,761,664 at Finding 88, a 3,821,568-byte
  (3.64-MiB, about 1.05-percent) reduction.  The 5,771-face 10088 prep-only
  run is essentially neutral at 236,707,840 bytes and succeeds in 15.47
  seconds.  Its isolated legacy-only two-depth comparison remains exactly
  correct but records a 294,780,928-byte process peak.  Because the audit
  frees one depth before building the other, that high-water number includes
  allocator-retained pages and later feature/second-prep allocations; it is
  not a count of simultaneously live arena nodes.

  `brep-audit --prep-only --prep-repeat=N` now performs complete repeated prep
  and unload cycles and labels every JSON record with repeat index/count.  Six
  normal-allocator 10057 cycles report peaks of 283,414,528, 388,243,456,
  409,079,808, 418,910,208, 424,792,064, and 428,048,384 bytes.  Growth falls
  from roughly 100 MiB after the first unload to 3 MiB at the sixth.  Four
  `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=1` cycles plateau at 264,671,232, 296,349,696,
  303,284,224, and 303,538,176 bytes, but take roughly twice as long.  This,
  plus sanitizer-clean logical teardown, implicates glibc per-thread arena
  retention/fragmentation in the remaining RSS trend rather than live
  `BBNodeArena` ownership.  The retained RSS still matters at production
  scale; forcing one malloc arena is diagnostic evidence, not the solution.

  The next representation decision should compare a reclaimable larger region
  with a compact indexed or build-then-pack runtime tree and must measure both
  live bytes and process high water.  Do not introduce a global BREP block
  cache that retains the largest model indefinitely.  Continue smaller
  topology/span/reference/vector/cache-streaming wins as well, since large
  top-level STEP assemblies accumulate them.

  A 15-second CPU profile explains the 229.89-second seam shard independently
  of this memory work.  The primary Cobb geometry has only six faces/spans;
  SurfaceTree build, BBox work, and BVH traversal consume less than one percent
  of the sample.  `brep_expansion_add` consumes 45.76 percent and
  `brep_expansion_scale` 25.09 percent, with exact Bezier restriction,
  blossoming, and fold determinant certification taking most of the balance.
  The fixed 128-component expansions do not malloc.  Near-tangent seam rays
  make cheap predicates inconclusive, so exact restriction is repeated during
  subdivision and contact certification.  Ordinary production and trace
  stacks both contain this work.  The test magnifies it by running 1,248 BREP
  shots, 416 implicit shots, 32 BREP preps, and thirteen auxiliary corpora;
  trace mode also runs prepared and legacy equivalence paths.  Nevertheless,
  the prepared production cost is a real defect, not merely test overhead.

  Establish an already-prepared, non-trace Cobb benchmark before optimization.
  Gate exact expansion behind conservative control-hull/double and outward-
  interval decisions, retaining it for the genuinely ambiguous tangent/fold/
  seam residue.  Measure regular, near-grazing, and exact-tangent populations
  separately and preserve fixed workspaces, no ray-time malloc, stage counters,
  selected/fallback populations, crack handling, and every correctness oracle.


Root-centered seam continuation and prepared throughput (Finding 90):

  `rt_brep_ray --throughput-only` now prepares one inward-bowed Cobb sphere
  once and times ordinary production shots separately from one untimed trace.
  It covers a regular diameter, 100T, 2T, and 1.1T grazing chords in both
  directions, and the exact zero-chord tangent.  Timing is informational; the
  gate is correctness, reversal symmetry, and proof provenance.  It is
  registered as `rt_brep_ray_throughput` and does not rebuild geometry inside
  a timed loop.

  The first fixture exposed two distinct failures in the old outside-span
  continuation certificate.  The 2T and 1.1T analytic sphere chords should
  each have one material interval, but the BREP path retained only one
  endpoint and then rejected its Newton continuation because a depth-24 box's
  conservative ray-t hull overlapped the existing endpoint.  A 100T case was
  worse: the certificate disabled adaptive correction, clipping, and
  Krawczyk termination over the complete extension.  It visited 82,013 boxes,
  produced 40,960 ambiguous terminals, overflowed the 64-box trace ledger by
  40,896, and spent several seconds before failing closed.  The six-span Cobb
  BVH considered two spans and excluded four; it was not responsible.

  The replacement proves the complete local root population without deeper
  global subdivision.  The certificate rectangle now includes the known
  existing UV as well as the reflected seed, edge point, and Newton
  continuation.  Both endpoint parameters are normalized into its outward
  interval reparameterization.  Along their strongest separating coordinate,
  centered boxes are limited to 20 percent of the endpoint separation and
  then contracted by powers of two.  Outward interval restriction plus strict
  Krawczyk inclusion proves exactly one root in each disjoint box.  The
  outward-rounded Krawczyk images are converted to rational surface ray-t
  hulls; each must contain its own representative and the two hulls must be
  disjoint.

  The two full Krawczyk boxes induce at most a 5-by-5 U/V grid over the local
  extension.  Cells covered by either unique-root box need no further work.
  Every other cell is recursively excluded by coordinate and vector Bernstein
  hulls.  The search is depth first in a 64-entry fixed stack, with an 8,192-
  box budget and the existing depth-24 arithmetic limit.  Any failed root
  certificate, interval overlap, third root, inconclusive complement,
  arithmetic failure, or exhausted budget rejects the repair.  There is no
  ray-time allocation.  Successful telemetry records two isolated/root boxes,
  Krawczyk attempts/availability/certification, complement boxes/exclusions,
  depth, and workspace.

  On the release fixture the 100T, 2T, and 1.1T cases require respectively
  2, 5, and 7 Krawczyk attempts.  Each complement consists of 13 cells, all
  excluded without further splitting.  Both ray directions now return one
  segment; the exact tangent returns none and invokes no continuation.  One
  representative optimized run measured approximately 1.47 ms/ray for the
  regular chord, 3.36 ms for 100T, 4.38 ms for 2T, 4.28 ms for 1.1T, and
  2.77 ms for the tangent.  These are not acceptance thresholds, but replace
  the former multi-second continuation behavior with bounded millisecond
  work.

  The full bowed 416-position matrix retains all 16 authorized below-envelope
  repairs, zero below-envelope leaks, zero reversal inconsistencies, and a
  maximum continuation proof of 15 boxes.  The complete current `--seam-only`
  shard still takes 233.94 seconds and reports 52 failures in unfinished
  prepared contact/oblique selection expectations; many of those diagnostics
  already report the correct production `2 hits / 1 segment`, and cases with
  zero continuation calls cannot be attributed to this certificate.  Keep
  this nonzero broad-shard result explicit rather than treating the 416-ray
  submatrix as the entire test.

  Focused optimized tests pass 11/11: directed, pole, throughput, interval,
  local-root, cleanup, grazing-root, trim interval/classifier, source union,
  and fold.  ASan/UBSan with leak scanning disabled passes directed, pole,
  throughput, and fold 4/4.  `git diff --check` is clean.

  A fresh 20-second, 199-Hz profile confirms the remaining seam cost.  Exact
  expansion addition accounts for 47.10 percent and scaling for 22.66
  percent.  During the early classifier family, 78.68 percent of complete
  stacks are in prepared surface proof work: 45.40 percent beneath ordinary
  production shots and 33.00 percent beneath the duplicate trace shots.
  `brep_fold_graph_determinant_signed` and
  `brep_fold_regular_graph_contract` repeatedly perform exact Bernstein
  restriction.  The next performance increment should stage ordinary outward
  interval contraction/exclusion ahead of this exact fold-graph tier and
  retain exact arithmetic only for residue that remains inconclusive.  It
  must preserve every fold/contact theorem and fail closed; merely imposing a
  low work cap would trade time for crack leaks.


Ordinary fold-graph contraction before expansion (Finding 91):

  The first exact-arithmetic performance increment is implemented.  Every
  regular-graph contraction now tries the same interval-Newton step with
  ordinary outward-rounded interval Bernstein evaluation and restriction
  before invoking expansion-valued evaluation.  A strict ordinary exclusion
  is authoritative because its input coefficients and every de Casteljau,
  derivative, quotient, and range operation enclose the exact values.  An
  ordinary contraction replaces the current coefficient enclosure only with
  a sound restricted enclosure.  Any unavailable, nonfinite, zero-containing,
  or noncontracting result remains inconclusive and executes the former exact
  expansion path unchanged.  Thus the new tier can avoid work but cannot turn
  an ambiguous contact into a miss or a material interval.

  Shot telemetry records ordinary contraction attempts, contractions, strict
  exclusions, and exact fallbacks.  Their accounting is checked by the
  prepared-once throughput fixture.  A new exact tolerance-contact family
  shoots the inward-bowed Cobb seam at clearance exactly T in both directions,
  requires the production answer to remain one segment, and requires the
  ordinary contraction tier to execute.  Its representative trace performs
  36 ordinary attempts, accepts 29 contractions, and retains seven exact
  fallbacks.  It visits 62 surface boxes from two candidate spans out of six;
  this is a fold/contact arithmetic workload, not a BVH workload.

  With no competing test process, one optimized run measures approximately
  1.47 ms/ray for the regular diameter, 3.16 ms for the 100T chord, 3.88 ms
  for 2T, 4.02 ms for 1.1T, 2.69 ms for the tangent, and 51.57 ms for exact
  seam contact.  The exact-contact number is still far from a production
  target.  The focused fold, grazing-root, and throughput gates pass.  The
  complete interval matrix also passes in 177.20 seconds; that exhaustive
  combinatorial arithmetic test is a correctness gate, not a production
  latency measurement.  ASan/UBSan with leak scanning disabled passes the
  directed, pole, throughput, and fold gates 4/4.

  Earlier representative whole-test timings fell from 0.23 to 0.18 seconds
  for the fold fixture, 11.20 to 6.83 seconds for grazing-root, and 13.45 to
  9.44 seconds for directed rays.  These are useful stage-level improvements,
  but the exact-contact trace shows the next bottleneck clearly.  Exact
  coefficient expansions are still constructed eagerly for every candidate
  span in `brep_surface_coefficients_init`, before ordinary hull rejection
  knows whether they will be needed.  The next increment should split cheap
  coefficient/enclosure construction from lazy exact expansion construction,
  retain exact arithmetic as the fail-closed authority for ambiguous residue,
  and measure how many candidate spans and boxes actually request it.


Lazy exact ray-plane coefficients (Finding 92):

  Ray-plane coefficient construction is now split into a mandatory cheap tier
  and an on-demand expansion tier.  The cheap initializer forms scalar
  coefficients, outward binary64 coefficient intervals, ray-parameter
  numerators, weights, and their error bounds.  It leaves the fixed expansion
  arrays uninitialized.  A retained span requests the exact tier only when a
  singular-boundary proof, terminal exact refinement, or fold certificate
  actually consumes it.  The initialized/available state is per-shot stack
  data; there is no allocation, shared mutable cache, or lifetime change.

  This is a scheduling change, not a weaker proof.  The exact initializer
  recomputes the same error-free coefficient expansions as before and
  intersects them with the already-valid ordinary intervals.  Every exact
  consumer still requires `expansion_available`; failure remains an
  inconclusive whole-ray fallback.  Ordinary hull exclusion can now discard a
  span before exact construction.  If ordinary isolation proves and orients a
  regular root without reaching terminal refinement, that candidate likewise
  never constructs expansions.

  The audit also removed six dead exact builds.  The continuation certificate
  reparameterizes only ordinary scalar/interval coefficient data, so its
  source expansions had always been discarded.  Regular-pair, regular-root,
  tight-event, edge-joint, and seam-source-union checks likewise consumed only
  scalar/interval data after separately invoking any exact local-root
  certificate they required.  Those initializers now explicitly request the
  cheap tier.  Reparameterized coefficient records explicitly reset exact
  state so later code cannot mistake discarded arrays for initialized data.

  Trace telemetry records exact coefficient requests, successful
  availability, failures, and spans that complete without a build.  The Cobb
  throughput fixture requires requests to equal available plus failures and
  requires every family to avoid at least one build.  A regular diameter
  requests 0 and avoids all 6 span builds.  The 100T, 2T, 1.1T, and tangent
  families request 1 and avoid 5; exact seam contact requests 2 and avoids 4.
  All requests are available and no build fails in this fixture.

  Relative to Finding 91's uncontended optimized sample, one representative
  run changes regular diameter from about 1.47 to 0.62 ms/ray, 100T from 3.16
  to 2.60 ms, 2T from 3.88 to 3.23 ms, 1.1T from 4.02 to 3.39 ms, tangent from
  2.69 to 2.11 ms, and exact seam contact from 51.57 to 48.67 ms.  The normal
  ray improves about 58 percent; the exact-contact path improves only about 6
  percent because its dominant work is later exact fold-corridor restriction.

  Ten focused optimized gates pass: directed, pole, throughput, local-root,
  cleanup, grazing-root, trim interval/classifier, source union, and fold.
  The complete interval arithmetic matrix passes again (222.19 seconds on a
  variable-load run).  Contact-trim passes.  The non-isoparametric shard
  retains its already-recorded six conservative prepared-selection failures;
  all six production answers remain the correct two hits and one segment.
  ASan/UBSan with leak scanning disabled passes directed, pole, throughput,
  fold, and contact-trim 5/5 with no diagnostic.

  The next seam-latency step is inside `brep_trace_fold_certificates`.
  Exact contact still constructs exact corridor and strip restrictions and
  their transformed coefficient nets before the ordinary contraction screen.
  Stage ordinary restriction/linear transformation and the complete corridor,
  boundary-existence, and strip proofs first, then replay the unchanged exact
  path only when the ordinary enclosure is inconclusive.  Do not cap or skip
  the exact residue: that would exchange latency for seam cracks.


Ordinary fold-graph determinant screening (Finding 93):

  Each weak-coordinate slab in `brep_fold_graph_determinant_signed` now
  restricts the two ray-plane Bernstein surfaces with ordinary outward
  intervals and forms their determinant before constructing expansion-valued
  restrictions.  A direct strict determinant sign is authoritative.  If the
  determinant is not yet signed, one ordinary interval-Newton contraction
  encloses the regular graph; the original ordinary determinant is restricted
  to that graph enclosure and tested again.  A strict exclusion or common
  strict sign can therefore finish the slab without exact expansions.  Every
  unavailable, nonfinite, zero-containing, or otherwise inconclusive result
  rebuilds the slab from the unchanged input and executes the former exact
  path.  No ambiguous result is interpreted as empty or signed.

  The screen is deliberately limited to one contraction.  Exact fallback
  rebuilds from the slab input rather than consuming the ordinary contracted
  coefficients, so further speculative contractions add work to every
  fallback.  The exact-contact Cobb trace proves three of twelve graph slabs
  after nine total ordinary contractions and sends the other nine slabs to
  exact arithmetic.  It retains the same 62 surface boxes, four isolated
  boxes, two fold-expansion requests, 36 outer fold contractions, one material
  segment in both directions, and no allocation at ray time.

  Trace telemetry records graph ordinary attempts, strict signs, exclusions,
  contractions, and exact fallbacks.  The prepared-once throughput fixture
  checks complete accounting and requires the exact-contact family to exercise
  the new screen.  The synthetic fold-graph bridge exposes the same telemetry
  and now requires an accounted ordinary graph certificate across every
  dyadic epsilon, equation scale, parameter orientation, and determinant sign;
  it no longer assumes that a correct proof must have followed the exact
  contraction implementation.

  `--throughput-contact-only` selects the exact-contact family, performs 32
  fixed prepared production shots, and reports both wall and process CPU time.
  This diagnostic mode is intentionally not a registered timing threshold.
  An interleaved A/B against a preserved exact-only library measured about
  47.14 versus 50.68 process milliseconds per shot before the host throttled.
  Hardware counters provide the more stable comparison: three prepass runs
  averaged 8.226 billion cycles for the complete 32-shot process versus 8.778
  billion for exact-only, about 6.3 percent fewer cycles.  The prepass retired
  about 0.4 percent more instructions, consistent with replacing a smaller
  amount of dependency-heavy expansion arithmetic with ordinary interval
  work.  This is an incremental gain, not a claim that the roughly 50-ms
  contact path is production-ready.

  A separate experiment replaced the existing fixed-expansion addition with
  Shewchuk's fast expansion sum, first directly and then with explicit
  compression, using the primary paper and reference software descriptions at
  `https://people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~jrs/papers/robustr.pdf` and
  `https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~quake/robust.html`.  Direct use raised the contact
  sample from roughly 49 to 94 ms; compression improved that to roughly 66 ms
  but remained slower and changed expansion populations.  These small,
  cancellation-heavy fixed arrays do not exhibit the workload assumed by that
  otherwise sound general algorithm, so the arithmetic experiment was fully
  reverted.  Optimize reuse and scheduling of exact restricted nets before
  revisiting their primitive representation.

  The warnings-as-errors build succeeds.  Eleven focused release gates pass
  11/11: directed, pole, prepared throughput, local root, cleanup,
  grazing-root, trim interval/classifier, source union, fold, and contact trim.
  The exhaustive interval gate passes in 149.64 seconds.  Rebuilt ASan/UBSan
  with leak scanning disabled for the established host-runtime limitation
  passes directed, pole, prepared throughput, fold, and contact trim 5/5 with
  no diagnostic.


Complete-axis exact restriction elision (Finding 94):

  `brep_expansion_surface_restrict` no longer runs an expansion-valued blossom
  along a parameter direction whose requested interval is the complete
  `[0,1]` domain.  After the unchanged equation power-of-two normalization,
  it converts the corresponding normalized source control interval directly
  into the intermediate expansion interval.  A complete second direction
  likewise selects the intermediate control interval directly.  Noncomplete
  directions execute the former blossom exactly.  Every output still obtains
  expansion bounds, intersects them with the same ordinary outward restriction,
  and is rescaled outward when the caller requests original equation scale.

  The complete-domain test uses the existing `DBL_MIN` endpoint convention to
  satisfy the warnings-as-errors floating-equality policy.  Treating a
  subnormal displacement from an endpoint as complete can only retain the
  larger source domain and widen the enclosure; it cannot exclude a root or
  create a strict sign.  The change is valid for every supported order and is
  not specialized to Cobb geometry.  Graph and strip subdivisions benefit
  especially because they restrict only the weak direction while retaining
  the complete regular direction.

  The interval corpus now adds exact-dyadic lower and upper coefficient
  oracles for complete-U, complete-V, and complete-both restrictions at orders
  2, 4, 8, and 16.  All 1,020 new coefficient enclosures contain their exact
  results below expansion capacity.  The complete exhaustive interval matrix
  passes in 166.01 seconds.  The ordinary production/proof matrix passes 11/11
  and rebuilt ASan/UBSan passes directed, pole, prepared throughput, fold, and
  contact trim 5/5 with no diagnostic.

  Against a preserved Finding-93 binary, three interleaved hardware-counter
  samples average 6.804 billion cycles for the fixed 32-shot exact-contact
  process versus 8.013 billion, a 15.1-percent reduction.  Retired instructions
  fall from about 7.700 to 7.242 billion, or 5.9 percent.  Unprofiled fixed-shot
  samples fall from roughly 45--46 to 39 ms per production contact while
  retaining identical 62-box, root, fold, graph, fallback, and segment
  populations.

  A fresh call-graph profile moves exact restriction beneath
  `brep_fold_graph_determinant_signed` from 24.64 to 13.66 percent of total
  sampled cycles and complete graph certification from 31.48 to 21.51 percent.
  Aggregate exact restriction remains the largest stack at 46.84 percent
  because the now-exposed corridor/strip, Krawczyk-contraction, localization,
  and terminal restriction calls still blossom noncomplete domains from their
  full source.  Next stage an ordinary complete corridor/boundary/strip proof
  or reuse/split already restricted exact nets; do not spend effort on
  OpenNURBS point evaluation while profiles identify Bernstein restriction as
  the dominant cost.


Deferred exact fold-strip construction (Finding 95):

  `brep_trace_fold_certificates` formerly restricted and transformed both the
  candidate corridor and its complementary strip before testing corridor
  uniqueness or root existence.  A strip exclusion can authorize publication
  only after those two prerequisites, so the eager strip net was dead work on
  every candidate that failed either one.

  The proof now constructs only the exact corridor source and transformed net
  initially.  It runs the unchanged corridor and graph tests, followed by the
  unchanged boundary-existence test for a unique corridor.  Only when the
  corridor is unique and either expansion Krawczyk or boundary existence has
  proved a root does it construct and transform the exact strip net.  The
  final publication rule still requires that same root proof, corridor
  uniqueness, and a successful strip exclusion.  A required but unavailable
  strip remains an inconclusive whole-ray fallback.  This is proof scheduling,
  not new authority or a work cap.

  Trace telemetry now records strip-build attempts, successful availability,
  and builds avoided after a corridor decision.  The synthetic affine-fold
  ratchet requires two builds, two available nets, and zero avoidance when its
  two fold roots complete.  The prepared-once throughput fixture requires the
  exact-contact family to avoid at least one build and checks build accounting.
  Its contact trace records corridor `1/0/0`, graph `1/0/1`, boundary
  `0/0/0`, strip-build `0/0/1`, strip `0/0/0`, and localization `0/0/0`.
  It retains two candidate spans of six, 62 subdivision boxes, four isolated
  boxes, fold contraction `36/29/0/7`, graph interval `12/3/0/9/9`,
  uncertified fallback reason 6, and the correct one-segment legacy result in
  both directions.

  Five interleaved hardware-counter comparisons against a preserved eager-
  strip binary average 6.252 billion versus 6.906 billion cycles for the
  complete fixed 32-shot process, about 9.5 percent fewer.  Instructions fall
  from about 7.244 to 6.829 billion, or 5.7 percent.  Uncontended current
  samples are about 35--36 process milliseconds per contact ray rather than
  roughly 39--40 ms for the eager binary.  This is still not production-ready;
  the graph and corridor paths continue to recompute noncomplete exact
  restrictions from their full source nets.

  The warnings-as-errors build succeeds.  Eleven focused release gates pass
  11/11 in 14.93 seconds: directed, pole, throughput, local root, cleanup,
  grazing root, trim interval/classifier, source union, fold, and contact trim.
  The exhaustive interval enclosure matrix passes with all 1,020 complete-axis
  identity checks.  Rebuilt ASan/UBSan with leak scanning disabled passes
  directed, pole, throughput, fold, and contact trim 5/5 in 91.93 seconds with
  no diagnostic.

  At this checkpoint the full seam shard reproduced the 52 prepared
  contact/oblique authority failures recorded at Finding 90, while all
  affected production shots returned their correct one-segment answer through
  conservative legacy fallback.  A complete run of the preserved eager-strip
  binary reproduced the same 52 failures and exactly the same 274/142
  selected/uncertified population, 18 certified seam pairs, 16 repairs,
  event-ledger counts, isolation maxima, and defect-corpus results.  That A/B
  result established that deferred strip construction did not cause the
  failures.  Finding 96 repairs their independent continuation-ownership cause
  without changing the strip proof or weakening fallback.


Candidate-specific seam population screening (Finding 96):

  The 52 remaining prepared failures were not missing surface roots or failed
  continuation solves.  In the affected oblique contact cases, two different
  source roots each produced a locally valid closure/continuation candidate.
  The physical-event resolver counted both before asking whether the other
  retained roots could form the complete physical population for that
  particular outer source/continuation interval.  The later uniqueness check
  therefore rejected the whole ray even though only one candidate could own
  the inner contact lobe.  Successful cases already had one candidate.

  A candidate-specific necessary-condition screen now runs after each bounded
  continuation solve but before it contributes to the authoritative candidate
  count.  It accepts only the two root-population forms already consumed by
  the unchanged final theorem:

    * every other retained root is a finite incident grazing witness at the
      same edge event, using the final theorem's model-tolerance/ray-length and
      coordinate-roundoff bound; or
    * exactly two other roots are transverse mathematical trim hits/misses on
      the other incident face and one prepared span, form an ordered
      ENTER/LEAVE pair, and lie strictly inside this candidate's outer
      source/continuation interval with the final theorem's t roundoff.  At
      most one trim miss is allowed; an explicitly declared contact closure
      requires exactly one hit and one miss.

  This screen grants no ray-intersection authority.  It can only discard an
  impossible candidate and thereby force fallback if no unique candidate
  remains.  The survivor still executes the existing local-root/tube proof,
  complete terminal-box and root ownership, connected source union, edge-gap
  and correspondence proof, material-sector test, ordering, minimum-segment
  check, and atomic physical-event publication.  No angle, crack, model, or
  numerical tolerance was increased.  Trace telemetry records population
  screens and rejections independently from accepted continuation candidates.

  The focused oblique trend now certifies 16 supported rays and leaves all six
  deliberately unsupported controls fail-closed.  Components 0.0001 through
  0.25 in both directions permanently require two screened candidates and one
  structural rejection; the component-0.5 forward case has the same observed
  disambiguation, while its reverse and both component-1 rays remain naturally
  unique.  The non-isoparametric opposite-face gate certifies all 16 cases.
  A new `--seam-authority-only`/`rt_brep_ray_seam_authority` shard combines the
  transformed classifier-invariance and oblique trend gates so this authority
  can be diagnosed without running the complete 416-position bowed matrix.

  The complete optimized release matrix on the final source passes 21/21 in
  174.22 seconds.  The full seam shard passes in 150.23 seconds and restores
  the prepared-production ratchet: 280 selected, 136 conservative uncertified
  fallbacks, and 24 certified seam transactions.  The bowed matrix retains all
  16 below-envelope repairs, zero below-envelope leaks, zero reversal
  inconsistencies, and the analytic material partitions.  Thus all 52 prior
  prepared contact/oblique failures are eliminated without converting an
  unsupported case into authority.

  Rebuilt ASan/UBSan initially terminated every process in LeakSanitizer with
  the host-runtime diagnostic that leak scanning does not work under ptrace;
  even the independent one-second fold arithmetic test showed it.  With leak
  scanning disabled for that established environment limitation, seam
  authority, non-isoparametric contact, contact-trim, and fold pass 4/4 in
  204.55 seconds with no ASan or UBSan diagnostic.  The full release run and
  focused uncontended authority run remain the performance references; the
  sanitizer timings include four-way instrumentation contention.  After the
  final finite-distance fail-closed guards, a rebuilt sanitizer authority run
  also passes 1/1 in 193.32 seconds without a diagnostic.

  Correctness is now green, but exact-contact latency remains a production
  defect.  The six-span Cobb tree contributes less than one percent of its
  profile; exact expansion add/scale and repeated noncomplete Bernstein
  restrictions dominate.  Continue with already-restricted net reuse and
  stronger inexpensive certified exits.  Keep that local arithmetic work
  separate from the large-STEP missing object/face/span BVH problem.


Shared exact endpoint restrictions (Finding 97):

  A fresh prepared-once contact profile confirms that exact Bernstein
  restriction remains the local hotspot after strip scheduling and seam
  correctness.  Of about 6,000 sampled cycles, expansion addition has 39.05
  percent self time and expansion scaling 18.66 percent; about 53.05 percent
  of all cycles descend through the per-coefficient blossom.  Surface-tree
  construction/traversal remains about one percent.  This is independent from
  the missing object/face/span BVH that dominates large imported objects.

  The general exact curve restriction evaluated an independent O(n^2)
  blossom for each of n output coefficients.  When the restricted interval
  touches parameter 0 or 1, all outputs are instead one boundary of a single
  de Casteljau triangle and can share O(n^2) expansion lerps in total.  The
  new endpoint kernel retains every triangle boundary value, uses the same
  fixed expansion add/scale operations, and requires no parameter division.
  `brep_expansion_surface_restrict` selects it only when one coordinate is
  complete and the other touches an endpoint; all interior and two-coordinate
  restrictions retain the general blossom.  Both routes use one factored
  expansion-bound/ordinary-bound intersection and the unchanged power-of-two
  normalization/rescaling.  The implementation remains allocation-free.

  The exact dyadic restriction oracle now covers left and right endpoint
  slabs in both parameter directions at square orders 2, 4, 8, and 16.  These
  add 1,360 coefficient enclosures to the full interval matrix, raising its
  complete-axis/endpoint identity population from 1,020 to 2,380.  A new
  `--restriction-only`/`rt_brep_ray_restriction` shard runs the endpoint 1,360
  independently; it passes in 0.04 release seconds and 0.32 ASan/UBSan seconds
  with expansion high-water three.

  A controlled ten-process hardware-counter A/B disabled only the endpoint
  dispatch while retaining the same source and oracle.  The general blossom
  averaged 899,665,821 cycles, 372,382,130 instructions, and 212.41 ms of task
  time.  The shared triangle averages 152,428,492 cycles, 59,760,221
  instructions, and 35.65 ms: about 83 percent fewer cycles/task time, 84
  percent fewer instructions, and a 5.96x kernel speedup.  Both paths pass all
  1,360 exact dyadic enclosure checks and report the same expansion-capacity
  high-water of three; bitwise bound identity is not required or claimed.

  This is a qualified general kernel improvement, not yet the exact-contact
  solution.  The Cobb contact fixture remains about 34--35 CPU ms/ray with
  identical two-of-six candidates, 62 boxes, four isolated boxes, fold/graph
  counters, fallback provenance, and one material segment.  Its remaining
  expensive restrictions are predominantly interior intervals, so the next
  exact stage must reuse/split already restricted immutable nets or derive a
  division-free shared interior transform.  Do not hide loss of tightness by
  accepting a wider proof or by shifting cases to legacy fallback.

  The final optimized release matrix passes 22/22 in 173.33 seconds; the full
  seam shard passes in 149.54 seconds.  The focused restriction, throughput,
  fold, affine, non-isoparametric, and seam-authority set passes 6/6 in 36.79
  seconds.  Rebuilt ASan/UBSan with leak scanning disabled passes restriction,
  throughput, and fold 3/3 in 3.14 seconds with no diagnostic.  Temporary perf
  recordings created for the profile were removed from `/tmp`; they are
  reproducible from the registered prepared-once and restriction shards.
