2026-09-29 UNCERTIFIED FIRST-FAILURE ATTRIBUTION CHECKPOINT
----------------------------------------------------------

The broad production fallback code
`RT_BREP_PREPARED_FALLBACK_UNCERTIFIED` remains unchanged.  It now carries an
orthogonal, decision-neutral cause: unresolved tangent/contact, coefficient
expansion unavailable, exact restriction failure, exact-refinement budget
exhaustion, corrector failure, Krawczyk uniqueness failure, or unknown.  The
isolation loop assigns the cause at each unresolved terminal box and preserves
the first cause on the ray; the explicit early tangent/contact rejection
records its own cause.  Neither the cause nor its telemetry is consulted by a
solver or publication decision.

With `LIBRT_BREP_PROFILE=1`, the profiler now aggregates ray population, sunk
prepared time, candidate spans, subdivision boxes, isolated boxes, and
Krawczyk boxes for each cause.  On
`nurbs_csg.g/recursive_child_linkage_complex_subtractions.s`, Release, one CPU,
cache disabled, 128 square, the exact result SHA-256 remains
`99a0e4d8e5888703d53b9a8f77e30c9c1dc6ae7ddee3b46b1b9ded621ad6ee22`.
The selected, `UNCERTIFIED`, and `EVENT_CLASS` populations remain 2,611, 3,894,
and 261.  All 3,894 uncertified rays have actionable causes: 288 corrector
failures (584.254 sunk ms, 2,028.661 us/ray) and 3,606 Krawczyk uniqueness
failures (6,104.754 sunk ms, 1,692.943 us/ray).  The unknown population is zero,
so attribution and actionable attribution are both 100.00 percent.

That controlled run assigns 99.98 percent of 7,219.816 shot ms.  Prepared work
is still the production blocker at 7,176.789 ms, or 99.40 percent: span/
isolation consumes 4,452.327 ms (61.67 percent) and physical event work
2,451.403 ms (33.95 percent).  The attribution therefore sharpens the next
performance experiment without changing the prior conclusion: conservative
preflight must avoid inevitable prepared rejection before this work is spent.

The incremental Release `rt` and `rt_brep_ray` build passed.  The complete
focused matrix then passed 43/43 with
`ctest -C Release -j 4 --output-on-failure -R
'^(rt_brep_|brep_audit_|rt_cache_)'` in 26.65 wall seconds.  The next checkpoint
is prep-time support metadata plus a conservative candidate preflight that may
only trigger whole-ray fallback when rejection is inevitable.


2026-10-05 GTA02 WHOLE-ASSEMBLY PREP DIAGNOSIS
-----------------------------------------------

The new `gta02-mme01_asm.g:GTA02_MME01_ASM` workload contains 306 B-Reps,
19,336 faces, 117,767 unique Bezier spans, 718,542 SurfaceTree leaves, 844,748
cell/span bindings, and 256,246 correction-risk cells at SurfaceTree depth 3.
The first cold-cache 128-square, 24-worker measurements were:

  main:          GETTREE 10.900 wall / 197.516 CPU seconds;
                 SHOT     0.121 wall /   0.875 CPU seconds
  branch routed:GETTREE 137.395 wall / 2241.080 CPU seconds;
                 SHOT     0.737 wall /  14.297 CPU seconds
  branch legacy:GETTREE 145.658 wall / 2250.190 CPU seconds;
                 SHOT     0.085 wall /   0.781 CPU seconds

Routed and branch-legacy images are identical.  Main differs in 3,170 pixels,
but no difference is a silhouette disagreement.  The GETTREE numbers above
were subsequently found to be invalid wall-time evidence: per-object prep
logging was serialized through PowerShell, and time blocked in an inner log
was charged to the enclosing routing phase.  One-leaf B-Reps consequently
reported roughly nine seconds of "routing" work.  Retain the image and shot
observations, but use the corrected native-redirection measurements below for
prep conclusions.

Prep profiling identified a concrete architectural cause.  Routing prep built
an exact restricted rational-normal hull independently for every cell/span
binding.  In the original zero-pack profile, routing-cell construction alone
used 614.843 CPU seconds out of 674.281 prep CPU seconds.  Four repeated
four-face B-Reps each have 53,068 leaves, 15,202 unique spans, and 53,068
bindings, although only 2,050 cells per object carry correction risk.  Thus the
cost is duplicate restriction work, not a fundamental cost of shallow
SurfaceTrees or of the grazing theorem.

A parameter-domain union in the prepared span hierarchy and a fixed-stack
overlap visitor removed repeated face-wide span scans.  With that index active
and binding hulls temporarily replaced by unique full-span hulls, cold
depth-3/zero-pack prep took 10.215 wall / 109.969 CPU seconds, compared with
main's 10.900 / 197.516.  This is direct evidence that main-comparable prep is
realistic.  Full-span hulls are not an acceptable final certificate: the
status-2 polycurve throughput fixture reported 48 small output differences.

Computing the exact restricted hull lazily in fixed ray-local storage restored
those outputs but reduced the focused routed/legacy throughput ratio to
0.1065 (about 4015.7 versus 427.5 microseconds per batch).  That experiment has
been removed.  Computing the rational-normal polynomial once per unique span
and restricting that shared polynomial for each binding reduced the next GTA
run to 81.798 wall / 214.719 CPU seconds; routing-cell construction accounted
for 145.717 CPU seconds.  Total CPU is consequently near main, but this extra
work is concentrated in a few large B-Reps and does not obtain main's prep
parallelism.  The safe default retains that shared-polynomial path and passes
`rt_brep_ray_status2_polycurve_throughput`: exact output, 10/10 positive routes,
and a latest 0.9981 routed/legacy CPU-throughput ratio.

An exact rectangle-reconstruction hierarchy was also tested experimentally.
After accounting for intentionally overlapping and nested
SurfaceTree padding it covered every mapped binding in the status-2 fixture
with zero fallback and preserved exact output.  On GTA, however, the first 89
completed objects already mapped 700,398 bindings using 621,760 splits; the run
was stopped after more than three minutes.  That elapsed time is not a valid
comparison: the run also selected directional-cache depth three and used the
serializing prep logger.  The node count is still useful architectural
evidence--it is almost one split per binding, plus recursive vector sorting and
coefficient copies--so this representation has not been selected for
production.  It remains disabled by default under
`LIBRT_BREP_EXPERIMENTAL_NORMAL_TREE`; a controlled timing would be required
before making a stronger performance claim.

The required production design is therefore a genuinely bounded, shallow
normal representation per unique span, not an exact reconstruction of every
cell rectangle.  Bindings may reference a small conservative cover and use
fixed ray-local refinement only for the inconclusive population.  Its node
count must be capped independently of binding count, with the current exact
cell-local decision retained as the differential oracle.  Ray traversal
remains fixed-workspace and allocation-free; overflow remains fail-closed.  The
present 8 MiB pack allowance is per B-Rep and must also become a scene-wide or
otherwise globally bounded budget.


2026-10-05 CORRECTED GTA02 PREP AND PARALLEL NORMAL CHECKPOINT
--------------------------------------------------------------

Native process redirection, `LIBRT_CACHE=off`, SurfaceTree depth 3,
directional depth 0, and a zero-byte routing-pack cache give the controlled
cold-prep baseline.  Clean main takes 10.900 wall / 197.516 CPU seconds.  Before
the changes below, exact shared restriction took 24.861 / 251.141 and the
first-order normal enclosure with direct Bernstein evaluation took 19.090 /
159.250.  Thus the branch did not have a fundamental CPU-work regression in
the shallow-tree configuration; it had poor effective parallelism and a long
object-level tail.

A low-overhead native-redirection prep profile located that tail.  First-order
shared-normal construction accounted for 72.090 seconds summed across B-Reps.
Four imported B-Reps, each with 53,068 mapped bindings and 15,202 unique spans,
accounted for 48.889 seconds of that sum.  Each object's span loop was serial,
so the four workers remained busy for roughly 12.1--12.4 seconds after most of
the tree-walk pool had drained.  This explains the apparently contradictory
159.250 CPU / 19.090 wall result versus main's 197.516 / 10.900: the branch did
less aggregate work but exposed far less concurrency.

Two exact arithmetic implementation costs were also removed.  Tensor-product
Bernstein multiplication now computes each rational interval weight once per
product instead of repeating interval division in the innermost coefficient
loop, and interval multiplication selects only the endpoint products capable
of attaining the extrema.  The exhaustive interval test remains exact and
passes.  Unique-span normal construction now partitions spans with
`bu_parallel` for B-Reps having at least 1,024 spans.  Workers write disjoint
binding records and aggregate only counters atomically; ray-time behavior and
the exact hull are unchanged.

With exact shared restriction still the default, two current GTA runs take
12.083 wall / 250.203 CPU seconds and 12.520 / 256.109.  This is within roughly
11--15 percent of clean-main wall prep and meets the initial within-20-percent
gate without relaxing grazing accuracy.  The focused recursive/concurrent
`bu_parallel` and prepared-cache tests pass.  The timing-sensitive status-2
throughput gate passes alone at a routed/legacy ratio above 0.96; as expected,
running that microbenchmark concurrently with seven other tests invalidates
its timing gate and is not a performance result.

An opt-in second-order enclosure is available under
`LIBRT_BREP_EXPERIMENTAL_SECOND_ORDER_NORMAL`.  It evaluates the normal and its
first derivatives at each binding center and adds a globally certified
Hessian remainder.  It performs no shot-time allocation.  Three cold GTA
trials with parallel normal prep take 8.473 / 154.984, 8.546 / 155.922, and
8.635 / 159.328 wall/CPU seconds; the median is about 21 percent faster than
clean main in both measures.  The status-2 fixture passes 10/10 positive routes
and its parameter-map oracle.  On the 128-square GTA view it restores the exact
124-ray directional-trigger population and produces the established
branch-legacy SHA-256
`C5956D892B36BECF1B4B67B53FF4D0E8556A3CA2699CEE0B6A21252E08BD7F2F`.
It remains experimental until broader differential and sanitizer coverage is
complete.

The remaining GTA shot-time variation is now clearly a direction-cache issue,
not prep.  The current 64-slot thread-local object cache serves 306 B-Reps;
worker scheduling changed otherwise equivalent profiled runs from 618 to 2,541
cache misses and from 0.311 to 0.649 seconds of SHOT wall time.  A bounded
shared immutable per-object/per-direction cache is the next production task.
It must preserve no-allocation shot behavior, safe publication, depth identity,
and fail-closed replacement.


2026-10-05 SHARED DIRECTION CACHE AND PACK-PREP ROOT CAUSE
---------------------------------------------------------

The 64-entry thread-local direction cache has been replaced by four fixed
prep-owned entries per B-Rep.  The key contains the prepared-object generation,
exact ray direction, selected depth, and cache format.  Readers are lock-free
and pin an immutable completed entry; a single nonblocking builder publishes an
entry and bounded round-robin replacement skips pinned entries.  Builder
contention, saturation, or construction failure falls back to the exact scalar
path.  All entry storage is allocated during prep, and ray tracing performs no
cache allocation.

On the controlled GTA02 128-square, 24-worker, depth-3, directional-depth-0,
zero-pack workload, three profiled runs each had exactly 45 direction-cache
misses instead of the former scheduling-dependent 618--2,541.  SHOT wall times
were 0.262, 0.273, and 0.284 seconds with 1.953, 2.234, and 2.281 CPU seconds.
Every run retained exactly 124 grazing triggers and produced SHA-256
`0FD2E65C53C30235109B18FF02A46FA23F90D6E99E94BEE7F152FD809054D816`,
which is also the branch-legacy result.  The shared cache fixes the miss
instability, but it does not remove the dominant optional prepared work: the
124 attempts consume approximately 0.96--1.04 aggregate seconds and only nine
complete and publish.

The apparently alarming GTA prep regression was isolated to the optional
routing cell/span pack cache, not to shallow SurfaceTrees or the grazing
certificate itself.  A current native-redirection prep profile with the former
8 MiB-per-B-Rep default took 67.805 wall / 1174.520 CPU seconds.  Routing prep
summed to 1105.031 seconds and eagerly constructed 193,537 packs across 304 of
306 B-Reps; 21 objects constructed at least 3,000 packs.  Each pack restricts
an interval control net to a cell and builds a 15-node subpatch bbox/normal
hierarchy.  The nominal allowance was independently available to every object,
so it was neither a scene-wide memory bound nor demand driven.

The identical profile with a zero-byte pack budget took 13.131 wall / 267.234
CPU seconds, with routing prep summing to 83.161 seconds.  An unprofiled render
after making zero the production default took 12.769 / 265.438 seconds in
GETTREE and 0.261 / 2.063 in SHOT and reproduced the branch oracle above.
Clean main's corresponding checkpoint is 10.900 / 197.516 in GETTREE and
0.121 / 0.875 in SHOT.  Current branch prep is therefore about 17 percent
slower in wall time, not six times slower, and remains inside the initial
20-percent gate.  The separately gated second-order enclosure has already
shown that faster-than-main prep is possible, although it is not yet qualified
as the default.

The former pack cache remains available explicitly through
`LIBRT_BREP_ROUTING_PACK_CACHE_BYTES`.  Its status-2 routing test now requests
8 MiB so the opt-in implementation remains covered.  It is disabled by default
until it has scene-bounded ownership and demonstrates useful amortization.  A
current opt-in GTA render took 65.854 / 1180.500 seconds in GETTREE and produced
shaded hash `48D0A4BE65A09C6A3BF0D75700834475DD72254A298D244D3CD2C7737F500B8F`,
not the zero-pack shaded hash.  Follow-up isolated this from routing semantics:
pack-on routed and pack-on legacy produce that same hash, while zero-pack
routed and zero-pack legacy produce the same alternate hash.  Material-
independent surface-normal renders are byte-for-byte identical with packs on
and off (`4C82AD02E94724ECBBBC519737F7888B80815762F1C9CDC8B372FAACC8CD2D37`).
All 272 shaded differences remain foreground and form coherent material-color
changes.  GTA contains many reported coincident overlaps, so changing parallel
prep duration changes claimant/material ordering without changing the primary
surface geometry.  This is an assembly overlap-determinism issue, not evidence
that the routing pack changed a B-Rep hit.  Same-prep-mode routed-versus-legacy
and material-independent products are the valid routing comparisons.

The prep outlook is consequently not a fundamental blocker.  The production
performance blocker is now shot time: the zero-pack routed sample is about
2.2 times main and 3.4 times branch legacy.  The next optimization must reject
the expensive, usually unsuccessful optional prepared transactions earlier;
more eager packing cannot address that dominant cost.


2026-10-05 ADAPTIVE GRAZING BUDGET AND CLEAN GTA02 RECHECK
----------------------------------------------------------

The preceding shot-time conclusion was traced to a fail-closed workspace
limit, not to unavoidable prepared work.  The 64-box directional grazing
budget exhausted on 124 GTA02 rays.  That exhaustion conservatively requested
124 optional prepared transactions, of which 115 failed after consuming about
1.01 aggregate seconds.  Raising the fixed-stack adaptive budget to 256 left
only two triggers and two 9.2-millisecond optional attempts.  At 1,024 boxes,
all 15,377 directional probes complete with zero ambiguity, overflow, trigger,
or optional transaction.  They visit 33,053 refined boxes--only about 46 more
than the 256-box run--and consume 229.084 aggregate milliseconds.

This is not a uniform SurfaceTree depth increase.  GTA02 still prepares at
SurfaceTree depth 3 and directional-cache depth 0.  The 1,024 bound applies
only to ray-visible adaptive subdivision of a span whose cached normal hull is
inconclusive.  The workspace is fixed stack storage; neither the shared
direction cache nor grazing certification allocates during a shot.  The
profiled 1,024-box render reports 1.17188 CPU / 0.10561 wall seconds in SHOT
and preserves the approved branch image.

Three clean, OS-redirected unprofiled routed samples use trials 2, 3, and a
fresh uniquely named trial 4; an earlier trial-1 image was discarded after an
interrupted shell-redirection experiment reused its output name.  Their
medians are 13.20277 wall / 277.672 CPU seconds in GETTREE and 0.10351 wall /
1.20312 CPU seconds in SHOT.  Three interleaved legacy-only samples have
medians 13.06442 / 274.781 in GETTREE and 0.07860 / 1.04688 in SHOT.  Every
valid run has 60,372 intersections, 35,616 hits, 24,756 misses, and SHA-256
`0FD2E65C53C30235109B18FF02A46FA23F90D6E99E94BEE7F152FD809054D816`.
Thus routing does not materially change prep, while current routed shooting is
about 14.9 percent more CPU time and 31.7 percent more wall time than branch
legacy on this short all-core sample.  Against clean main's 0.875 CPU / 0.12118
wall seconds, it uses more aggregate CPU but finishes faster in wall time.

The clean recheck also sharpens the prep diagnosis.  With packs disabled, the
306-object profile attributes 83.161 aggregate seconds to routing-cell prep;
78.633 seconds of that are shared normal-envelope construction.  SurfaceTree
construction sums to 56.894 seconds and edge preparation to 45.493 seconds.
Those phases parallelize into approximately 13 seconds of wall time.  The old
per-object 8 MiB pack policy alone raised routing-cell prep to 1,105.031
aggregate seconds and wall GETTREE to 65.854--67.805 seconds.  Current exact
prep is therefore near, although this three-trial median is 21.1 percent above,
main's 10.89963-second wall result.  Earlier exact samples were 11--15 percent
above main, and the separately gated exact second-order enclosure remains
faster than main at an 8.546-second median.  The evidence supports further
optimization and qualification; it does not indicate a fundamental prep-time
barrier.

The 1,024-box setting retains genuine correction coverage.  The status-2
routing and polycurve-throughput tests, direct proxy/polycurve/revolution/
torus routes, all ten routing-truth audits including the representative
63-face correction replay, `rt_brep_route_direct`, and
`rt_brep_profile_histogram` pass.  Compact per-binding normal metadata,
retained-memory measurement, broader STEP/FRAME coverage, and sanitizer work
remain open.
